# Endpoint pointwise convergence for the Schrodinger equation in higher dimensions

Coordinate `EN-110-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-C-PH-M-0181` | `PM-080.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Endpoint-pointwise-convergence-for-the-Schrodinger-equation-in-higher-dimensions-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Endpoint-pointwise-convergence-for-the-Schrodinger-equation-in-higher-dimensions-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We resolve the Sobolev endpoint of Carleson's pointwise convergence problem in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3. For initial data in $`H^{n/(2(n+1))}(\\mathbb R^n)`$, the free Schrödinger evolution converges almost everywhere to the initial data as $`t\\downarrow0`$. The evolution is defined by removing Gaussian regularization on one full-measure spatial set, uniformly over the interval $`0\\lt t\\lt 1`$.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the Sobolev endpoint of Carleson's pointwise convergence problem in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3. For initial data in $`H^{n/(2(n+1))}(\mathbb R^n)`$, the free Schrödinger evolution converges almost everywhere to the initial data as $`t\downarrow0`$. The evolution is defined by removing Gaussian regularization on one full-measure spatial set, uniformly over the interval $`0\lt t\lt 1`$.

- **claim-endpoint-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-m-main)): For every fixed integer $n\geq3$ and every complex-valued $f\in H^{s_n}(\mathbb R^n)$, there is a full-measure set $E_f$ of Lebesgue points of $f$ and a function $v(x,t)$, $x\in E_f$, $0\leq t<1$, such that $v(x,\cdot)$ is continuous, $v(x,0)=f^*(x)$, and $$\label{m:eq-uniform-Gaussian} \lim_{a\downarrow0}\sup_{0<t<1}\|U_af(x,t)-v(x,t)\|=0 \qquad(x\in E_f).$$ In particular the finite Gaussian limit $$u_f(x,t)=\lim_{a\downarrow0}U_af(x,t)$$ exists for every real $0<t<1$ on this one set, and $\lim_{t\downarrow0}u_f(x,t)=f^*(x)$.
  - Explanation: At the critical Sobolev index in every dimension at least three, the Gaussian-regularized free evolution converges uniformly in time to a continuous representative that equals the initial datum at Lebesgue points of a single full-measure set.
- **claim-weighted-multilinear-kakeya** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-kakeya)): Let $d\geq2$. For $j=1,\ldots,d$, let $\mathcal T_j$ be a family of infinite cylinders of radius $h$ in $\mathbb R^d$, with unit direction vectors $e(T)$. Suppose $$\|\det(e(T_1),\ldots,e(T_d))\|\geq\vartheta>0 \quad(T_j\in\mathcal T_j).$$ For nonnegative weights $c_j(T)$, $$\label{p:kakeya-equation} \int_{\mathbb R^d}\prod_{j=1}^d \left(\sum_{T\in\mathcal T_j}c_j(T)\mathbf1_T\right)^{1/(d-1)} \leq C_d\vartheta^{-1/(d-1)}h^d \prod_{j=1}^d\left(\sum_{T\in\mathcal T_j}c_j(T)\right)^{1/(d-1)}.$$ For lattice cubes of side $a\le h$, the same right-hand side bounds $a^d$ times the corresponding sum of cube suprema, after a fixed dimensional enlargement of every cylinder.
  - Explanation: Weighted endpoint multilinear Kakeya inequality for transverse families of cylinders, with polynomial dependence on inverse transversality and a lattice-cube version.
- **claim-robust-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p-projection)): Fix $d\geq2$, $0<\alpha<d$, and $\rho>0$. There are positive constants $\eta_0,\gamma$ and $\Delta_0$ with the following consequence. If $0<\eta\leq\eta_0$, $0<\Delta<\Delta_0$, $A$ lies in a fixed unit ball, $$\Delta^{-\alpha+\eta}\leq\mathcal N_\Delta(A) \leq\Delta^{-\alpha-\eta},\qquad \mathcal N_\Delta(A\cap B(x,r)) \leq\Delta^{-\eta}r^\rho\mathcal N_\Delta(A) \quad(\Delta\leq r\leq1),$$ and $\nu$ is a probability law on unit kernel directions satisfying $$\label{p:projection-angular} \nu\{v:\|v\cdot w\|<r\}\leq\Delta^{-\eta}r^\rho \quad(\|w\|=1,\ \Delta\leq r\leq1),$$ then some direction in the support of $\nu$ has $$\label{p:projection-conclusion} \mathcal N_\Delta(\pi_{v^\perp}A') \geq\Delta^{-(d-1)\alpha/d-\gamma} \quad\text{for every }A'\subset A\text{ with } \mathcal N_\Delta(A')\geq\Delta^\eta\mathcal N_\Delta(A).$$ Fixed multiplicative constants and bounded affine normalizations in the hypotheses are allowed after reducing $\eta_0,\gamma$.
  - Explanation: Robust discretized hyperplane projection input: a nonconcentrated set and a nonconcentrated direction law yield a direction whose projections of all large subsets keep a positive power gain.
- **claim-packet-reproduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p-reproduction)): For the band-limited class in [p:conventions](#p:conventions), the following operations are uniform in the size of the packet family.* 1. *A polynomially decaying packet is an absolutely convergent sum of packets with the same fixed enlarged native Fourier support and arbitrarily rapid Schwartz decay about translated native centers. The coefficient at a translation $j\in\mathbb Z^{k+1}$ is at most $C_A s_T(1+\|j\|)^{-P'}$, where $A$ is the requested output decay order.* 2. *All fixed native derivative orders satisfy the corresponding envelope bounds. If $F$ has Fourier support in a fixed bounded set and $r>0$, then, for every sufficiently large fixed $L$, $$\label{p:sup-convolution} \sup_{\|u-z\|\leq C}\|F(u)\|^r \leq C_{r,L}\int \|F(y)\|^r(1+\|y-z\|)^{-L}\,\mathrm dy.$$* *Consequently integral packet estimates below also hold with unit-cube suprema, or with separate unit suprema for factors of a product. Output Schwartz orders may be chosen after a small loss parameter; the original exponent $P'$ and original smooth-profile derivative order stay fixed.
  - Explanation: Reproduction of polynomially decaying packets as sums of rapidly decaying packets, plus control of unit-cube suprema, uniformly in the size of the packet family.
- **claim-buffered-refined-decoupling** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-buffered-refined)): Put $d=k+1$, $R\geq2$, and $2\leq p\leq q_k$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\vartheta_0=\vartheta_0(d,p,\varepsilon)>0$ such that the following holds for $0<\vartheta\leq\vartheta_0$ and $\Lambda=R^\vartheta$, with a constant allowed to depend on $(d,p,\varepsilon,\vartheta)$. Work in a fixed $O(R)$ test box and one common padded time slab $S$ of length $O(R)$. Every tube core meets a fixed $O(R)$ enlargement of the test box; in particular the family has polynomial cardinality. Fine frequency labels lie in a bounded patch of a translated, bounded-overlap lattice of mesh $R^{-1/2}$; the allowed class of such lattices is closed under parabolic rescaling. A fixed $O_d(1)$ number of cap grids may be separated into colors. A labelled tube is the intersection with $S$ of $$\{(x,t):\|x-2vt-u\|_\infty\leq\Lambda R^{1/2}\}.$$ For $c\geq1$, $cT$ dilates the transverse width only, in the same common slab. Tubes of a fixed label have bounded overlap before clipping by a spatial box; equivalently, their intercepts are globally separated at scale $\Lambda R^{1/2}$ up to fixed multiplicity. Fix $1\leq a\leq10$ and $a+1/2\leq b\leq20$. Suppose the nonzero $F_T$ have comparable $L^p$ norms, $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\widehat F_T\subset \{(\xi,\tau):\|\xi-v\|\lesssim R^{-1/2},\ \|\tau+\|\xi\|^2\|\lesssim\Lambda/R\},\qquad \\|F_T\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^d\setminus aT)}\leq R^{-B}\\|F_T\\|_p,$$ where $B$ is a sufficiently large fixed number. Let $U$ be a union of $R^{1/2}$-cubes in the interior of the box and slab, each meeting at most $M\geq1$ of the *counted* tubes $bT$. Then $$\label{p:buffered-equation} \left\\|\sum_TF_T\right\\|_{L^p(U)} \leq C_{\varepsilon,\vartheta,d,p}R^\varepsilon M^{1/2-1/p}\left(\sum_T\\|F_T\\|_p^p\right)^{1/p}.$$ The same assertion holds for finitely many fixed cap-grid colors. The spatial boxes and tube strips may be translated; all overlap assumptions refer to the unclipped strips.
  - Explanation: Buffered refined decoupling in ambient dimension k+1 with a strict support-to-count margin, losing only an arbitrarily small power of the scale.
- **claim-arbitrary-auxiliary-weights** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-weighted)): Let $2<p\leq q_k$, and let $f_T$ be band-limited packets of scale $N$. For $b_T\geq0$, require $f_T=0$ if $b_T=0$, and set $B_b(z)^2=\sum_Tb_T^2w_T(z)$. Then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{p:weighted-equation} \int B_b(z)^{2-p}\left\|\sum_Tf_T(z)\right\|^p\,\mathrm dz \leq C_\varepsilon N^\varepsilon N^{k+2}\sum_Ts_T^p b_T^{2-p}.$$ The integrand is defined as zero where both the field and $B_b$ vanish. The estimate admits unit suprema. If all frequency labels lie within $C/N$ of a $\kappa$-dimensional affine plane, $1\leq\kappa\leq k$, the range extends to $2<p\leq q_\kappa$. In the zero-dimensional case there are only boundedly many frequency slots and the assertion holds for every fixed finite $p>2$.
  - Explanation: Weighted packet estimate with arbitrary auxiliary square weights, extended to larger exponent ranges for frequencies near lower-dimensional planes.
- **claim-local-linear** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-local-linear)): Let $V=\sum_Tf_T$ be either a band-limited or a smooth packet field of scale $N$, and let $B^2=\sum_Ts_T^2w_T$. For a ball $D$ of radius $N$ and any $z_0\in D$, $$\label{p:local-linear-equation} \\|V\\|_{L^{q_k}(D)}\leq C N^{k/2}B(z_0).$$ The same bound holds for the unit-supremum norm on $D$.
  - Explanation: Local linear estimate on a ball of the packet scale with no scale loss, in terms of the square envelope at one point.
- **claim-local-multilinear** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-local-multilinear)): Let $V_j$ and $B_j$ be as in [p:local-linear](#p:local-linear), with frequency labels in sets satisfying <a href="#p:transversality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p:transversality">[p:transversality]</a>. For fixed $p>p_k$, a ball $D$ of radius $N$, and arbitrary points $z_j\in D$, $$\label{p:local-multilinear-equation} \left\\|\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}\|V_j\|^{1/(k+1)}\right\\|_{L^p(D)} \leq C_p\delta^{-C_p}N^{k/2} \prod_{j=1}^{k+1}B_j(z_j)^{1/(k+1)}.$$ Separate unit suprema may be taken for the $k+1$ factors. In particular the constant has no $N^\varepsilon$ factor.
  - Explanation: Local multilinear estimate for transverse packet fields with polynomial dependence on inverse transversality and no scale loss.
- **claim-sampling-preserves-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-convolution)): Let $K'\geq K\geq1$, let a $K'$-cap lie in a $K$-cap, and let $T'$ be a child cell of scale $K'$. If $\mathcal G_K$ is the parent grid in that cap, then, for $P>k+2$ sufficiently large, $$\label{p:convolution-equation} \sum_{T\in\mathcal G_K}w_T(z)w_{T'}(z_T)\leq C_Pw_{T'}(z).$$ The same exponent $P$ occurs on both sides, independently of $K'/K$. If the sum is restricted to $d_T(z)>L$, $L\geq2$, it is at most $$\label{p:convolution-tail} C_P\left(L^{-(P-k-1)}+(K/K')^{P-k-2}\right)w_{T'}(z).$$ When $d_{T'}(z)$ is bounded, the second term can be omitted.
  - Explanation: Summing parent envelopes against child envelopes at parent centers preserves the same envelope exponent, independently of the scale ratio.
- **claim-weighted-hierarchy-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-hierarchy)): Take nested frequency grids at scales $K<K'$, rounded within fixed factors. Assemble all child fields in each parent cap, and localize them on its parent physical grid. The localizers can be chosen band-limited, with summable Schwartz tails and bounded sums, so that the resulting parent packets have amplitudes $a_T$ and satisfy $$\label{p:parent-weights} b_T^2\geq c\sum_{T'\text{ in the parent cap}}b_{T'}^2w_{T'}(z_T)$$ whenever the parent weights are chosen to dominate the displayed sum. For $2<p\leq q_k$, $$\label{p:hierarchy-equation} \sum_T K^{k+2}a_T^p b_T^{2-p} \leq C_\varepsilon(K'/K)^\varepsilon \sum_{T'}(K')^{k+2}a_{T'}^p b_{T'}^{2-p}.$$ It remains valid after retaining any subset of parent cells, at every step of a finite hierarchy. After modifying a child field, use its fresh amplitudes in the same fixed parent masks. On subtrees lying in a codimension-one strip at their final frequency resolution one may use $p=q_{k-1}$ at every transition; for $k=1$ any fixed finite $p>2$ is available. Products of the transition estimates give the complete finite-hierarchy estimate.* *Fixed polynomial weights on macroscopic boxes, and any fixed additional edge decay, are allowed. Their orders are chosen once, before the finite grid is chosen. Constants may depend on the finite grid; the required original profile regularity and decay order do not grow with its depth.
  - Explanation: Weighted packet quantities transport through nested frequency grids with only a small power loss in the ratio of scales, valid after deleting parents or modifying child fields.
- **claim-near-cauchy** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-near-cauchy)): Let $V=\sum_Tf_T$ be a band-limited field of scale $N$, and let $B^2=\sum_Ts_T^2w_T$. Suppose a finite set $Y$ of unit cubes has witnesses $z_Q\in Q$ such that $$B(z_Q)\leq1,\qquad \|V(z_Q)\|\geq N^{k/2}/R,\qquad R\geq1.$$ Then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{p:near-cauchy-equation} \|Y\|\leq C_\varepsilon R^{q_k+\varepsilon}\sum_Ts_T^2.$$ The conclusion holds with unit suprema and fixed enlargements of the witness cubes. The Fourier enlargement and original decay orders can be fixed before $\varepsilon$ is chosen.
  - Explanation: Count of unit cubes where a band-limited field is within a factor R of its pointwise Cauchy bound, with no loss depending on the packet scale.
- **claim-polynomial-start** (bound, [source](#stmt-p-polynomial-start)): Under the hypotheses of [p:near-cauchy](#p:near-cauchy), there is a fixed finite $M_k$ such that $$\label{p:polynomial-start-equation} \|Y\|\leq C(2+R)^{M_k}\sum_Ts_T^2.$$
  - Explanation: A finite polynomial starting exponent for the near-Cauchy count, which makes the exponent-infimum argument noncircular.
- **claim-frame-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-frame)): Fix $k\ge2$, $c>0$, and fixed packet bounds $C_0$. There is a finite integer $P_0$ such that, for every fixed $P\ge P_0$, there are a finite integer $J_0$ and a constant $C$, depending only on $k,c,C_0,P$ and the packet conventions, with the following property. Let $h\ge2$, let $V_1,\ldots,V_m$ be scale-$h$ packet sums on one time interval of length at most $C_0h^2$, and suppose their normalized compact profiles have derivatives through order $J_0$ bounded by $C_0$. Let $\Xi_i$ be their assigned frequency-label sets, with the fixed cap enlargements included. Assume bounded phase-space counting and $$\big\|\det((2\xi_1,1),\ldots,(2\xi_m,1))\big\|\ge\delta>0 \quad(\xi_i\in\Xi_i).$$ Use $P$ for the packet envelope exponent. Let $Y$ be a set of distinct unit cubes and, for every $Q\in Y$ and $i$, let $z_{i,Q}\in Q$ satisfy $$\|V_i(z_{i,Q})\|\ge A_i\ge h^c, \qquad B_i(z_{i,Q})^2=\sum_Ts_{i,T}^2w_T(z_{i,Q})\le1.$$ The points $z_{i,Q}$ need not agree for different $i$. Set $$H_i=A_i^{2/k},\qquad H_g=\Big(\prod_{i=1}^mH_i\Big)^{1/m}, \qquad D_i=h^{k+2}\sum_Ts_{i,T}^2.$$ Then $$\label{f:mainbound} \|Y\|\le C\delta^{-C}H_g^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^m\frac{D_i}{A_i^2}.$$ Only the finite number $J_0$ of profile derivatives is required.
  - Explanation: Transverse frame estimate counting unit cubes where k+1 transverse packet fields are simultaneously large, with separate witnesses and polynomial dependence on inverse transversality.
- **claim-reduction-exact-packets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-exact)): It suffices to prove Theorem <a href="#f:frame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="f:frame">14</a> for translated exact paraboloid extensions, with smooth data supported in bounded native frequency boxes, multiplied by a common Schwartz function of $t/h^2$ which is positive on the test interval and has compact Fourier support. All required seminorms of these exact packets can be fixed in advance.
  - Explanation: The frame estimate reduces to translated exact paraboloid extensions with smooth bounded-frequency data and a common compactly Fourier-supported time cutoff.
- **claim-clipped-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-smoothing)): Fix $C_3$ sufficiently large. There is a fixed $L$ such that, with $K_0=HS^L\le h$, each cap field $V_b$ admits a scale-$K_0$ packet representation with integrated square energy at most $CD$ and envelopes $e_b^2=\sum_{\gamma\in b}s_\gamma^2w_\gamma^{\rm work}$ satisfying $$\label{f:clipped} \sum_b\min(e_b^2(z),M_c)\le S^{o(1)}, \qquad M_c=H^{-k}S^{C_3},$$ outside $o(\|Y\|)$ witnesses. The work weight $w_\gamma^{\rm work}=(1+d_\gamma)^{-P_{\rm work}}$ has a fixed order $P_{\rm work}>P$, chosen in the proof; all subsequent levels of this subpower tree use that order. The envelope and profile orders are finite and independent of any subsequent scale-grid length. If $K_0>h$, the tree instead ends at the unchanged original scale-$h$ packets with their original order $P$; their square envelope is already bounded at the witnesses.
  - Explanation: On the subpower scale each cap field has a packet representation whose clipped square envelopes sum to a subpower quantity outside a negligible set of witnesses.
- **claim-finite-grid-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-f-selection)): On a fixed finite grid one can select ratios $r_{i,s}$, retain a subpower fraction of the high cubes, and retain packet masks with the following properties. Put $$\label{f:occupancy-size} M_{i,s}=\kappa_i(s)^{k+2}(r_{i,s}/A_i)^q.$$ At every selected scale the original selected amplitudes obey $a_T\asymp r_{i,s}b_T$, and $$\begin{aligned} Q_{i,s}&:=\sum_T\|T\|a_T^qb_T^{2-q}=\Lambda^{o(1)}D_i, \label{f:Qsat}\\ E_{i,s}&:=\sum_T\|T\|a_T^2=\Lambda^{o(1)}D_ir_{i,s}^{2-q}, \label{f:Esat}\\ \sum_{T\text{ active}}b_T^2&\le \Lambda^{o(1)}\|Y\|/M_{i,s}. \label{f:Bcharge} \end{aligned}$$ An active cell has at most $\Lambda^{o(1)}M_{i,s}$ eligible witnesses in its prescribed subpower enlargement. All witnesses are eligible in the power case and at strict ancestors of transition bins. Within a subpower transition bin $b$, eligibility means $e_b^2(z)\le\Lambda^{o(1)}M_c$.* *More generally, a collection of actual outputs in fixed masks whose weighted $q$ budget is at most $\Lambda^{-\varepsilon}D_i$, for fixed $\varepsilon>0$, has negligible root contribution. The same assertion holds for error summands in any later restricted tree.
  - Explanation: On a finite scale grid one selects amplitude-to-weight ratio classes and masks saturating the weighted budgets, with occupancy and discard control.
- **claim-limiting-profiles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-profiles)): After taking subsequences and finer finite rational grids there are nonnegative, nonincreasing, $k/2$-Lipschitz functions $f_i$ such that $$r_{i,s}=\Lambda^{f_i(s)+o(1)},\qquad f_i(e_i)=0.$$ In the power case $f_i(0)=\lim\log_h A_i$. In the subpower case $$\label{f:d-bounded} 0\le d_i(s):=f_i(s)+ks/2\le ke_i/2\qquad(s\le0).$$
  - Explanation: After subsequences the logarithmic amplitude ratios converge to nonnegative nonincreasing Lipschitz profiles vanishing at the fine endpoint.
- **claim-restricted-path-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-paths)): For the selected tree, and for each of the directional restrictions constructed below, one can draw a uniform root point $Z$ from a subpower fraction of $Y$ and then a path to a fine direction $\Omega_i$ in each field. The intermediate active cells lie within subpower normalized distance of $Z$. The fields’ paths may be drawn independently conditional on $Z$.* *For the reference cap square weights just defined, at a typical sampled label $$\label{f:cap-law} \Pr(\tau\mid Z)=\Lambda^{o(1)}b_\tau^2(Z).$$ Let $T_s(Z,\Omega_i)$ be the cell containing $Z$ in the disjoint scale-$\kappa_i(s)$ lattice tilted by $\Omega_i$. At a typical draw, $$\label{f:occupancy-law} \frac{\Pr(T_s(Z,\Omega_i)\mid\Omega_i)} {\Pr(Z\mid\Omega_i)}=\Lambda^{o(1)}M_{i,s}.$$ Typical means outside probability tending to zero on each fixed finite test, with any prescribed positive exponent tolerance.
  - Explanation: A normalized path law from a root point to fine directions exists whose cap probabilities and fiber occupancies match the reference square weights and occupancy scales up to subpower factors.
- **claim-conditioning-directions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-conditioning)): Inside a fixed cap $\tau_l$, let $C$ be a physical cell over which every descendant velocity has drift at most the endpoint width, and whose time range is at most the endpoint duration. Adding an independently redrawn descendant direction as conditioning changes the logarithmic information of position inside $C$ by $o(1)$ at a typical draw. The cell may depend on a different conditionally independent drawn direction. The conclusion also holds for subpartitions and subpower distortions. In the power case it holds for a direction from another field whenever its full weights have the same bounded drift on $C$.
  - Explanation: Conditioning on an independently redrawn descendant direction changes the information of position inside a cell by a vanishing amount.
- **claim-angular-test-broad-witnesses** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-angular)): Fix one field, a cap parameter $l$ away from the endpoints and the transition seam, and a finite mesh of $0<v\le u$ with $l+Cu<e_i$. Suppose that $$f_i(l)-f_i(l+v)\ge xv$$ on this mesh, for some $x>0$. For a number $\eta>0$ depending only on $x,k$, one may restrict the frozen tree so that, at typical $(Z,\tau_l)$, its new conditional direction law assigns mass at most $\Lambda^{-\eta v+o(1)}$ to every affine hyperplane strip of width $\Lambda^{-v}$ in the native frequency coordinates of $\tau_l$.* *At one arbitrarily small mesh precision $v_0>0$, the sampled active $l$ cells may also be required to have broad witnesses for their *original pre-modification* fields. Namely, there are $k+1$ $(l+v_0)$ cap groups, transverse throughout their supports with determinant at least $\Lambda^{-C_kv_0}$, each having magnitude at least $\Lambda^{-C_kv_0}r_{i,l}b_{\tau_l}(Z)$ at a point within native distance $\Lambda^{C_kv_0}$ of the cell.* *The high root set loses only a vanishing fraction, and Lemmas <a href="#f:paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="f:paths">19</a> and <a href="#f:conditioning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="f:conditioning">20</a> apply to the modified tree with the same recorded occupancy sizes. This construction is made for one fixed block at a time; no simultaneous assertion over all blocks is needed.
  - Explanation: Where a profile has negative slope, the tree can be restricted so directions do not concentrate near hyperplane strips, while broad transverse witnesses of the original fields are retained.
- **claim-ball-inflation** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-inflation)): Work inside a cap $\tau_l$ of the preceding lemma, and suppose $f(l+v)-f(l)=-xv+O(\varepsilon u)$ on the required finite mesh in $[0,2u]$. Put $d_0=1-2x/k$. For the isotropic cell $Q_v$ of radius $\Lambda^v$ in this cap’s native coordinates, define the sampled effective occupancy $$N_v=\Pr(Q_v(Z)\mid\tau_l)/\Pr(Z\mid\tau_l).$$ For $u\le v\le2u$ on that mesh, $$\label{f:inflation-occupancy} \log_\Lambda(N_v/M_l)\le(k+1)d_0v+O(\varepsilon u).$$ The error can be made arbitrarily small by taking the broad precision $v_0/u$ small, refining the finite mesh, and then taking the sequence limit. All spatial tests stay on one side of the transition seam.
  - Explanation: Upper bound on effective occupancy of isotropic balls within a cap, obtained by iterating multilinear Kakeya inflation over broad witnesses.
- **claim-binary-slope-rigidity** (classification, [source](#stmt-f-binary)): Every profile satisfies $$\label{f:binary-eq} f_i'(s)\in\{0,-k/2\}\quad\text{for almost every }s.$$
  - Explanation: Every limiting profile has derivative equal to zero or the full Cauchy slope at almost every parameter.
- **claim-midpoint-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-midpoint)): For almost every pair $l<a$ of full-slope points in one field, $(l+a)/2$ is a full-slope point. Consequently each field’s full-slope set is an interval modulo null sets. In the power case, if $a$ is a full-slope point in one field, then $a/2$ is a full-slope point in every other field, for almost every such $a$.
  - Explanation: Full-slope points are closed under midpoints, so each full-slope set is an interval modulo null sets, with a cross-field halving rule in the power case.
- **claim-cross-field-inflation** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-cross-field-inflation)): Let $K\le R\le K^2$ be two inverse widths in a fixed finite packet tree. At scale $K$, let $W_j$, $1\le j\le m=k+1$, be the original selected packet fields testing the root high set, with no outer smaller-inverse-width mask. Each $W_j$ is assembled from exactly its selected scale-$R$ descendants through the frozen bounded masks and scale-$K$ localizers; no omitted branch contributes to $W_j$. The witnesses below test these same masked fields $W_j$. Suppose their frequency labels, including fixed enlargements, are $\delta$-transverse. Write $\mathcal E_{j,K}=\sum_Ta_{j,T}^2w_T$ for their local square envelopes and $\mathcal E_{j,R}$ for the envelopes of their descendants at scale $R$ in the same frozen tree. Let $Y$ be a set of distinct unit cubes with separate field witnesses at thresholds $A_j$. Suppose that, on every $K$-ball meeting $Y$, $$K^{k/2}\mathcal E_{j,K}(z)^{1/2}/A_j\le U, \qquad 1\le j\le m,$$ at one, hence every, point $z$ in that ball, where $U\ge1$. Then for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$ and every $R$-ball centered at a surviving root point $z$, $$\label{f:cross-field-inflation-bound} \#(Y\cap B_R(z)) \lesssim_{\varepsilon,\mathcal G}\delta^{-C_\varepsilon}U^\varepsilon R^{k+1} \prod_{j=1}^m\bigl(\mathcal E_{j,R}(z)/A_j^2\bigr)^{1/k}.$$ The constant may depend on the fixed finite grid $\mathcal G$, but not on the aspect ratio $R/K$.
  - Explanation: One-step incidence bound from scale K to scale R for transverse fields, with no loss in the aspect ratio.
- **claim-vanishing-left-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-f-left-limit)): In the subpower case, $$\label{f:left-limit-eq} \lim_{s\to-\infty}d_i(s)=0 \quad\text{for every }i.$$ Consequently $f_i(0)=0$, including when $e_i=0$.
  - Explanation: In the subpower case the defect function tends to zero at minus infinity, forcing the profile to vanish at the origin.
- **claim-du-zhang-squared-input** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-dz-input)): Let $\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $S\ge1$. Suppose their counting function in every ball of radius $r\ge1$ is at most $\Gamma r^n$, and their number in any ball of radius $\sqrt S$ is at most $\lambda_0$. Then for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\sup_q\|\mathcal E g\|^2 \le C_\varepsilon S^{\varepsilon} \Gamma^{4/((n+1)(n+2))}\lambda_0^{\gamma_n} S^{\gamma_n}\lVert g\rVert_2^2. \label{d:dz-sup}$$ All fixed enlargements and translations of the cube lattice are allowed, with fixed changes of the constant.
  - Explanation: Squared form of the Du-Zhang fractal extension estimate for unit cubes with ball-growth and square-root-scale occupancy parameters.
- **claim-weighted-fractal-norm** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-refined)): Suppose $\mu$ is supported in a ball of radius $CS$, satisfies <a href="#d:ball-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:ball-growth">[d:ball-growth]</a>, and, in addition, $$\mu(B(z,\sqrt S))\le d\,m S^{n/2}, \qquad S^{-A_0}\le d\le1. \label{d:sqrt-occupancy}$$ Then $$\sum_q\mu(q)^\beta\sup_q\|\mathcal E g\|^2 \le C_{\varepsilon,A_0}\,m^\beta S^{n/(n+1)+\varepsilon}d^{\gamma_n}\lVert g\rVert_2^2. \label{d:weighted-fractal}$$ With $d=1$ no extra assumption beyond growth is needed. The estimate is homogeneous in $\mu$ and in the squared input norm.
  - Explanation: Weighted fractal norm estimate with an occupancy gain at the square-root scale, homogeneous in the measure and in the squared input norm.
- **claim-exact-fourier-layers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-d-layers)): For every prescribed finite collection of derivative and native spatial decay orders, a sufficiently high finite profile regularity in <a href="#d:array" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:array">[d:array]</a> gives a representation $$U(x,t)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^n}\int_\mathbb R \rho(k,u)e^{iu t/L^2}\mathcal E g_{k,u}(x,t)\,\mathrm du, \label{d:layer-representation}$$ where $\rho(k,u)\le C_A(1+\|k\|+\|u\|)^{-A}$ for any prescribed $A$. For every subarray $\mathcal A$, $$\lVert g_{k,u}^{\mathcal A}\rVert_2^2 \le C\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A}\|c_\alpha\|^2. \label{d:layer-subarray-energy}$$ Each label contributes an exact input supported within $O(L^{-1})$ of $\omega_\alpha+k/L$, with uniformly bounded native frequency seminorms. On a fixed native time range, its extension has all the prescribed spatial decay about the original trajectory after the factor $\rho$ is extracted. The shift $k/L$ and the time modulation are common to the entire layer and every subarray. For any fixed $\eta>0$, the portion $\|k\|>L^\eta$ can be made an arbitrarily negative power error in the array supremum, and hence in tests involving polynomially many cubes and polynomially bounded mass parameters.
  - Explanation: Compact arrays decompose into integrable families of exact extensions with rapidly decaying weights and subarray energy control by coefficient energy.
- **claim-packet-localization-slab** (theorem, [source](#stmt-d-packet-expansion)): Let $T\ge1$ and let $g$ have bounded frequency support. On a time interval of length $O(T)$, an exact extension can be localized to spatial boxes of side $T$ with summed input energies $C\lVert g\rVert_2^2$. The localization errors have arbitrarily high negative powers of $T$, with summable local squared budgets. No factor equal to the number of spatial boxes is needed.
  - Explanation: An exact extension on a time slab localizes to spatial boxes with summed input energies controlled and arbitrarily small negative-power errors.
- **claim-power-loss-compact-arrays** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-power-loss)): Let $U$ be a compact array of scale $L$, and let $\mu$ satisfy <a href="#d:ball-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:ball-growth">[d:ball-growth]</a> in a spacetime box of side $O(L^2)$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, with sufficiently many fixed profile derivatives, $$\sum_q\mu(q)^\beta\sup_q\|U\|^2 \le C_\varepsilon m^\beta L^{2n/(n+1)+\varepsilon}E(U). \label{d:compact-fractal}$$
  - Explanation: Weighted fractal norm for compact arrays with only an arbitrarily small power loss in the scale.
- **claim-strip-decoupling** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-strip-decoupling)): Group a field $V=\sum_\theta V_\theta$ by frequency-label bins of side $K^{-1}$. Suppose the field supports lie in fixed enlargements of these bins and within vertical Fourier distance $O(K^{-2})$ of the paraboloid. If the labels lie within $O(K^{-1})$ of an affine $j$-plane, $1\le j\le n$, and $2\le P\le q_j=2(j+2)/j$, then $$\lVert V\rVert_{L^P(Q)}^2 \le C_{\varepsilon,A}K^\varepsilon \sum_\theta\sum_{a\in\mathbb Z^{n+1}}\langle a\rangle^{-A} \lVert V_\theta\rVert_{L^P(Q+K^2a)}^2. \label{d:local-strip-decoupling}$$ The constituent fields may be arbitrary prescribed subfields with these supports. Without the strip hypothesis the same estimate holds with the factor $CK^n$ in place of $C_\varepsilon K^\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Squared-norm decoupling for fields whose labels lie near an affine plane, with a small power loss instead of the full dimensional loss.
- **claim-coarse-norm-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-transport)): Let $F=X_{K,v}$ with bounded $v$, and suppose $V(z)=K^{-n/2}e^{i\varphi(z)}V'(Fz)$ with $\|e^{i\varphi}\|=1$. For a fixed $a\in\mathbb Z^{n+1}$, $$\sum_Q\nu(Q)^b\lVert V\rVert_{L^P(Q+K^2a)}^2 \le C K^{2-(n+2)b}\sum_{r,s\in\mathcal R} \mathcal T_b(V',\nu_{a,r,s}), \label{d:transport-functional}$$ where $\mathcal R$ is a fixed finite set, independent of $Q,K,a$, and $$\nu_{a,r,s} =(\tau_{F(K^2a)+K^2(s-r)})_\# F_\#\nu, \qquad \tau_h(z)=z+h. \label{d:translated-measures}$$ Their translations have size at most $CK^2(1+\|a\|)$. The same conclusion holds with any prescribed subset of parent cubes on the left. If $\nu$ has growth parameter $m$, each child measure has parameter $CmK^{n+1}$. Direct pushforward transports <a href="#d:tube-moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:tube-moment">[d:tube-moment]</a> from scale $L$ to $L/K$ with the same $D$.
  - Explanation: Coarse functional transports under parabolic rescaling to translated pushforward measures with explicit power factors and preserved growth and moment parameters.
- **claim-codimension-two-strip-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-strip)): Fix $0<\alpha<1/8$. Suppose $g$ has bounded frequency support contained in an $O(S^{-\alpha})$ neighborhood of an affine $(n-2)$-plane, and $\mathcal Q$ is a collection of unit cubes in a ball of radius $S$ with at most $m_0r^n$ cubes in every radius-$r$ ball, $r\ge1$. Fix any $b$ with $\beta<b<2/n$. There is $c_0>0$, depending only on the fixed dimensional choices and independent of $\alpha$, such that for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\sup_q\|\mathcal E g\|^2 \le C_{\alpha,\varepsilon}m_0^b S^{n/(n+1)-c_0\alpha+\varepsilon}\lVert g\rVert_2^2. \label{d:strip-saving}$$ In particular, for $m_0=S^{o(1)}$ this is the saving $S^{n/(n+1)-c_0\alpha+o(1)}$. Finite disjoint subinputs are allowed, with their squared norms summed on the right.
  - Explanation: A strict power saving in the fractal extension bound when frequency support lies near an affine plane of codimension two.
- **claim-transverse-saving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-transverse)): Fix a bounded frequency region in $\mathbb R^n$. There are $c>0$ and $L_0<\infty$, depending only on this region and $n$, such that the following configuration is impossible for $L\ge L_0$. Let $\mathcal Q$ be distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $L^2$ in $\mathbb R^{n+1}$, with $$\label{t:forbidden-counts} \#\mathcal Q\ge L^{2n-c},\qquad \#\{q\in\mathcal Q:q\cap B(z,r)\ne\varnothing\} \le L^c r^n\quad(r\ge1).$$ Suppose $f_1,\ldots,f_{n+1}$ have $L^2$ norm at most one, frequency support in the fixed region, and $$\sup_q\|Ef_i\|\ge L^{-e-c} \quad(q\in\mathcal Q,\ 1\le i\le n+1).$$ It is impossible that every choice of one frequency from each support has direction determinant at least $L^{-c}$ in absolute value.
  - Explanation: A dense, ball-growth-controlled configuration of cubes where n+1 transverse exact extensions are all large is impossible at large scale.
- **claim-multilinear-polynomial-dependence** (bound, [source](#stmt-t-multilinear)): If $n+1$ bounded-frequency exact inputs are transverse with determinant at least $\nu\in(0,1]$, then, for each $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{t:multilinear-extension} \sum_{q\subset B_S}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1} \bigl(\sup_q\|Ef_i\|\bigr)^{2/n} \le C_\varepsilon\nu^{-M_\varepsilon}S^\varepsilon \prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\lVert f_i\rVert_2^{2/n}.$$ The same bound holds with the sum replaced by the integral of the product. For transverse families of tubes of width $\delta$ in a fixed bounded region, with nonnegative weights $w_i(T)$, the corresponding Kakeya bound is $$\label{t:kakeya} \int\prod_{i=1}^{n+1} \left(\sum_Tw_i(T)\mathbf1_T\right)^{1/n} \le C_\varepsilon\nu^{-M_\varepsilon}\delta^{n+1-\varepsilon} \prod_i\left(\sum_Tw_i(T)\right)^{1/n}.$$ Fixed enlargements are allowed. Subpower enlargements and $\nu=S^{-o(1)}$ give only subpower losses.
  - Explanation: Multilinear extension and Kakeya bounds with polynomial dependence on inverse transversality.
- **claim-pruned-incident-tubes** (construction, [source](#stmt-t-tubes)): A countersequence to Proposition <a href="#t:transverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="t:transverse">36</a> yields a set $\mathcal P$ of centers of distinct $L^{-1}$-scale cubes in a bounded region, satisfying $$\label{t:point-counts} \#\mathcal P=L^{n+o(1)},\qquad \#(\mathcal P\cap B(z,r))\le L^{n+o(1)}r^n \quad(L^{-1}\le r\le1),$$ and $n+1$ transverse weighted tube families of width $L^{-1+o(1)}$. Their weights satisfy $\sum_Tw_i(T)\lesssim1$. For every $x\in\mathcal P$ there is an incident subfamily of retained weight $w_i^*(x)=L^{-e+o(1)}$ whose normalized law satisfies $$\label{t:strip-law} \mathbb P\{\xi(T_i)\in N_{L^{-\alpha}}(H)\mid X=x\} \le L^{-c_1\alpha+o(1)}$$ for every affine $(n-2)$-plane $H\subset\mathbb R^n$ and every fixed rational $0<\alpha<1/8$. Here $c_1>0$ is fixed independently of $\alpha$, and the estimate is uniform in $x,H$ for each fixed $\alpha$.
  - Explanation: A countersequence yields a point set and weighted tube families whose conditional direction laws avoid strips near affine codimension-two planes.
- **claim-limiting-information-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-information)): After passing to a subsequence, the countably many information variables at rational block and prefix scales have a common limiting joint law. Their conditional informations are uniformly integrable and bounded in the limit. The chain rule is exact, and conditioning lowers information almost surely in this law. If, on a specified event and given $(Z,V)$, there are at most $L^{a+o(1)}$ possible labels for $Y$, then the limiting information $I(Y\mid Z,V)$ is at most $a$ on that event. Event indicators can be included in the joint law; finite systems of limiting inequalities of positive probability pass to finite scale with fixed positive slack.
  - Explanation: Countably many information variables have a common limiting law with exact chain rule and conditioning lowering information almost surely.
- **claim-block-additivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-additivity)): Put $J=F(s+t)-F(s)$ and $J_i=G_i(s+t)-G_i(s)$ for the selected references. The nonnegative costs $$\ell=I(X_{s+t}\mid C)-I(X_{s+t}\mid C,D),\quad \ell_i=I(X_{s+t}\mid C,T_i)-I(X_{s+t}\mid C,T_i,D)$$ satisfy $$\label{t:block-comparison} \sum_i(J_i-\ell_i) \le\sum_i I(Z_i\mid Z_{\widehat i},C,D) \le I(Z\mid C,D)=J-\ell.$$ For disjoint blocks or prefixes with the same bin list and the same exact tube conditioning, the sum of expectations of each cost is $O_n(b_*)$. Almost surely, for every choice of references, $$\label{t:replacement} F=\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}G_i,\qquad G_i'=G_i,\qquad G_i(1)=\frac n{n+1}.$$ For every coordinate subset $A\subset\{1,\ldots,n+1\}$, $$\label{t:subset-information} I((Z_i)_{i\in A}\mid C,D) =\sum_{i\in A}J_i+O_n\left(\sum_i\ell_i\right).$$ No unused coordinates are included in the conditioning in this formula.
  - Explanation: Block comparison with telescoping costs of total expected size controlled by the direction-bin scale, giving additive decomposition of the information profile.
- **claim-restriction-two-equations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-rank)): Let a basis of $\mathbb R^{n+1}$ consist of bounded vectors $v(\xi_i)$ with inverse norm $L^{o(1)}$. Normalize the $i$th coefficient of another bounded vector $v(\xi)$ to one, assuming it and its inverse are $L^{o(1)}$, and denote two of its other coefficient ratios by $a_j,a_k$. A ball of radius $L^{-u}$ in these two ratios pulls back, on the bounded frequency region, into an $L^{-u+o(1)}$-neighborhood of an affine $(n-2)$-plane. The same conclusion holds when both specified ratios are $L^{-u}$-small.
  - Explanation: A ball in two direction ratios pulls back to a neighborhood of an affine plane of codimension two.
- **claim-common-profile** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-common)): Almost surely all $n+1$ conditional profiles coincide: $$\label{t:common-profile} G_i=G_i'=G,\qquad F=(n+1)G,\qquad 0\le\dot G\le1,\quad G(1)=\frac n{n+1},\quad (n+1)G(s)\ge ns.$$
  - Explanation: All conditional information profiles coincide almost surely into one common Lipschitz profile with the stated endpoint behavior.
- **claim-robust-planar-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-projection)): Fix $0<d<1$ and $\rho>0$. There are $\varepsilon_{\rm pr},\eta_{\rm pr}>0$ such that, for sufficiently small $\Delta$, the following holds. Let $A\subset B(0,1)\subset\mathbb R^2$, and write $\mathcal N_\Delta$ for covering number at radius $\Delta$. Suppose $$\Delta^{-2d+\eta_{\rm pr}}\le\mathcal N_\Delta(A) \le\Delta^{-2d-\eta_{\rm pr}},\qquad \mathcal N_\Delta(A\cap B(z,r)) \le\Delta^{-\eta_{\rm pr}}r^\rho\mathcal N_\Delta(A)$$ for $\Delta\le r\le1$. If a probability $\sigma$ on projection lines obeys $\sigma(B(V,r))\le\Delta^{-\eta_{\rm pr}}r^\rho$ on the same range, then some $V\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma$ satisfies $$\mathcal N_\Delta(\pi_VA')\ge\Delta^{-d-\varepsilon_{\rm pr}} \quad\text{for every }A'\subset A\text{ with } \mathcal N_\Delta(A')\ge\Delta^{\eta_{\rm pr}}\mathcal N_\Delta(A).$$ The tolerance and gain may be decreased.
  - Explanation: Planar version of the robust projection theorem used in the block test.
- **claim-projection-block-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-block-test)): Fix $0<d<1$, $d_0>0$, and $\kappa_0>0$. A sufficiently small $\zeta>0$, depending on these parameters and fixed $n$, has the following property. Fix rational $[s,s+b]\subset[0,1]$, $b<1/8$, $b_*\ge b$, and $j\in\{2,3\}$. Let $D_0$ be the reference direction bins at precision $L^{-b_*}$, and $D_1$ additionally contain the bin of $T_1'$. The following cannot hold on an event of positive limiting probability:* 1. *$\|G(s+b)-G(s)-db\|\le\zeta b$, and $G(s+u)-G(s)\ge d_0u-\zeta b$;* 2. *every cost for $D_0,D_1$, the reference tubes, and $T_1'$, at the endpoint and the tested prefixes, is at most $\zeta b$;* 3. *$h_j(u)\ge\kappa_0u-\zeta b$.* *The prefix tests need only use a fixed finite rational grid in $0<u\le b$ containing $b$, of mesh a sufficiently small fixed multiple of $\zeta b$. All error constants and relative meshes are independent of $s,b,b_*$.
  - Explanation: Linear profile growth, small costs and angular nonconcentration cannot hold together on a block with positive probability.
- **claim-finite-angular-alternatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-angular-alternatives)): For fixed $d\in(0,1)$ and $d_0>0$, there is a fixed finite list of positive rational ratios, bounded away from zero, with this property. For every sufficiently small rational $b_0$, almost every path admits a length $b'$ from that list times $b_0$, and $j=2$ or $3$, satisfying the angular hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#t:block-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="t:block-test">44</a>, with spare slack, for all tested block lengths in $[b'/4,b']$. Each alternative has a fixed positive angular slope, tolerance, and finite relative mesh, independent of $b_0$.
  - Explanation: A finite list of scale ratios guarantees that at least one of two marginal directions is nonconcentrated across the tested block lengths.
- **claim-binary-derivative-alternation** (classification, [source](#stmt-t-alternation)): Almost surely $\dot G\in\{0,1\}$ almost everywhere, and $E=\{s:\dot G(s)=1\}$ agrees modulo null sets with a finite union of intervals. There is an interior full-to-empty transition. Consequently, for every fixed $\eta>0$, some rational block $[s,s+b]$ with $0<b<1/8$ satisfies, on an event of positive probability, $$\label{t:jump} G(s+b)-G(s)=b/2+O(\eta b),\qquad G(s+b/2)-G(s)=b/2+O(\eta b).$$
  - Explanation: The common profile has binary derivative almost everywhere with finitely many alternations, producing an interior full-to-empty transition.
- **claim-no-full-to-empty-block** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-no-jump)): For sufficiently small fixed $\eta>0$, the positive-probability event in <a href="#t:jump" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="t:jump">[t:jump]</a> is impossible.
  - Explanation: Polynomial partitioning rules out the full-to-empty block, completing the transverse saving.
- **claim-sparse-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-sparse)): There is $c_s>0$, depending only on the fixed dimension and packet boxes, with the following property. Fix $\tau>0$ and $L^\tau\le D\le L$. Let $U$ be a full-dimensional compact-profile array of scale $L$, with coefficient energy $\mathcal E=\sum_\alpha\|c_\alpha\|^2$. Let $\mu$ be a nonnegative measure on the fixed native time range, obeying $$\mu(B(z,r))\le mr^n\quad(r\ge1),\qquad \int\left(1+\frac{\|x-y_\alpha-2t\omega_\alpha\|}{L}\right)^{-M} \,\mathrm d\mu(x,t)\le\frac{mL^{n+1}}D$$ for every nonzero packet label, as in Definition <a href="#d:measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:measure">27</a>. For sufficiently many bounded profile derivatives, $$\label{s:estimate} \sum_{q\text{ unit}}\mu(q)^\beta\sup_q\|U\|^2 \le C_\tau m^\beta L^{2n/(n+1)}D^{-c_s}\mathcal E, \qquad \beta=\frac2{n+1}.$$ The same estimate holds for any fixed finite set of field derivatives if the original profiles have sufficiently many more derivatives. The exponent $c_s$ is independent of $\tau$; the constant and the required finite differentiability may depend on $\tau$. Restrictions to submeasures and fixed enlargements of the native time range are allowed.
  - Explanation: Sparse packet arrays obey the fractal estimate with a fixed power saving in the sparsity parameter.
- **claim-geometric-alternative** (classification, [source](#stmt-s-simplex)): For a fixed sufficiently large $C_0$, either all active centers lie within $C_0/K$ of an affine hyperplane, or $n+1$ active bins have direction determinant at least $cK^{-n}$ throughout their exact supports. A common frequency shift does not affect this alternative.
  - Explanation: Active bins are either near a hyperplane or contain a transverse simplex with controlled determinant.
- **claim-broad-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-broad)): There exists $c_b>0$, depending only on the fixed frequency box and $n$, such that if $K\le l^{c_b}$, then in every box of side $O(l^2)$, $$\label{s:broad-bound} \sum_{Q\text{ broad}}\nu(Q)^\beta\lVert V\rVert_{L^P(Q)}^2 \lesssim m_0^\beta l^{2n/(n+1)-c_b}\mathcal E_0.$$ Here $\nu(B(z,r))\le m_0r^n$ for $r\ge1$, and the input squared norms of the total field and every subfield are at most $C\mathcal E_0$. The fixed $C$ may affect the implicit constant, but the gain is independent of packet differentiability and decay orders.
  - Explanation: Broad cubes enjoy a fixed positive power gain from the transverse saving.
- **claim-weak-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-w-weak)): Fix $n\geq3$, and use the compact arrays, measure conditions and fixed time range of [d:measure](#d:measure). There are constants $$p>2,\qquad \sigma>0,\qquad 0<2a<1-\beta, \qquad \beta=\frac2{n+1},\quad e=\frac{n^2}{n+1},$$ depending only on the dimension and the fixed packet conventions, for which the following holds. Suppose that the array has coefficient energy $E$, that $1\leq D\leq L$, and that the measure has ball and packet-moment parameter $m$ as in [d:measure](#d:measure). Suppose also that $$\mu(B(z,1))\leq m\chi,\qquad 0<\chi\leq1,$$ and that its support is in a box of side $O(L^2)$ within the fixed time range. For every $g>0$, $$\label{w:eq-weak} \mu\{z:\|U(z)\|\geq \chi^a gL^{-e}\} \leq C_{\mathrm{ind}}mL^{2n}g^{-p}D^{-\sigma}E^{p/2}.$$ The constants are independent of $L,D,m,\chi,g$ and the particular array. The assertion also holds for a subarray with its original native profiles.
  - Explanation: Lossless weak estimate for compact arrays with unit-density and sparsity parameters, proved by two inductions.
- **claim-angular-induction** (bound, [source](#stmt-w-angular)): Assume the outer induction hypothesis in [w:weak](#w:weak) and the active range <a href="#w:eq-active" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="w:eq-active">[w:eq-active]</a>. Fix a subarray of energy $E_0\leq1$. In every cube of a fixed spacetime grid of side $R^4$, prescribe some of its frequency bins of side $1/R$. Suppose their centers are covered by $O((1+\log X)^{C_0})$ sets, each of one of the following types:* 1. *a strip of width $O(R^{-1})$ about an affine $(n-2)$-plane;* 2. *the $O(R^{-1})$-neighborhood of $$\{v\in H\cap B: \|Q_H(v)\|\leq R^{-4}\},$$ where $H$ is an affine hyperplane, $B$ is a fixed bounded region, and $Q_H$ has coefficient norm one in orthonormal coordinates on $H$ with bounded origin.* *The cover and the prescribed bins may depend on the macro cube. Let $U_{\mathrm{sel}}$ denote the resulting prescribed-bin sum on that cube. For any fixed $c>0$, $$\label{w:eq-angular} \mu\{\|U_{\mathrm{sel}}\|\geq c\lambda\} \leq C C_{\mathrm{ind}}R^{-c_2} mL^{2n}g^{-p}E_0^{p/2}+C_NmL^{-N}.$$ Here $N$ can be any prescribed fixed exponent, given sufficiently many initial profile derivatives. The exponent $c_2>0$ depends on the dimensional choices in <a href="#w:eq-ab" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="w:eq-ab">[w:eq-ab]</a>, not on $u$ or $C_0$.
  - Explanation: Selected bins trapped near codimension-two strips or small quadratic sublevel sets contribute a fixed power gain to the induction.
- **claim-heavy-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-w-heavy-reduction)): There is a subset $Y'\subset Y$ with $\mu(Y')\geq cM_Y$ such that, at each point of $Y'$:* 1. *the heavy-bin field has modulus at least $\lambda/2$;* 2. *the heavy-bin centers are not contained in a strip of any fixed large constant times $R^{-1}$ about an affine $(n-2)$-plane, nor in a single quadratic neighborhood of the form in [w:angular](#w:angular);* 3. *every heavy bin supplies at least $W/2$ of its weighted coefficient square mass from trajectories within distance $X^{C_w}L$.* *In particular some $n$ heavy bins have direction vectors $(2\omega,1)$ whose $n$-volume is at least $R^{-C}$ throughout the bins.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical large level set reduces to a positive-fraction subset supported by broad heavy bins.
- **claim-incidence-wall** (bound, [source](#stmt-w-wall)): The preceding level set satisfies $$\label{w:eq-incidence-mass} N_YW^{n/(n-1)}\leq X^C, \qquad N_Y\geq X^{-C}L^n.$$ Moreover, a subset of $Y'$ of mass comparable to $N_Y$ lies within distance $X^C$ of the zero set of a polynomial of degree at most $X^{C_{\mathrm{part}}}$ in tube coordinates.
  - Explanation: Incidence mass is controlled and a positive fraction of the level set lies near a polynomial wall of controlled degree.
- **claim-regular-wall-pieces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-w-regular-wall)): Let $Z$ be the square-free polynomial wall. It has a semialgebraic exceptional subset of dimension at most $n-1$ and complexity $X^C$ such that, outside its $C\rho$-neighborhood, every relevant point of $Z$ has a local graph coordinate with bounded slope and second and third graph derivatives bounded by a fixed power of $\rho^{-1}$. The estimates are independent of the polynomial coefficients. The mass of the discarded neighborhood in tube coordinates is at most $X^C\rho L^n$.
  - Explanation: The square-free wall has regular graph pieces with derivative bounds outside a small semialgebraic exceptional set.
- **claim-line-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-w-line-tests)): For any prescribed large $A$, choose a fixed $C_6$ sufficiently large. For every label, outside polynomially many macroscopic time intervals of total length at most $X^{-A}$, an incidence with any eligible $p$ satisfies $$\label{w:eq-line-tests} \|v_j-\nabla\phi(p)\cdot\bar v\|\leq X^{C_6}/L, \qquad \|\nabla^2\phi(p)[\bar v,\bar v]\|\leq X^{C_6}/L, \qquad v=(2\omega,1).$$ The exponent bounding the number of intervals is independent of the sizes of $A,C_6$.
  - Explanation: Incident lines are tangent to the wall with second-order contact outside negligible parameter intervals.
- **claim-affine-plate-covering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-w-geometry)): A subset $Y_0\subset Y'$ with $\mu(Y_0)\gtrsim M_Y$ is contained in a union of at most $X^C$ affine hyperplane plates, each of thickness $X^C$ in tube coordinates.
  - Explanation: A positive-fraction subset lies in few affine hyperplane plates of controlled thickness.
- **claim-flattening-energy-budgets** (bound, [source](#stmt-w-flatten)): For each physical time bin of length $h^2$, including its fixed enlargement, $$\label{w:eq-flat-unweighted} \sum_{\mathrm{bin}}s_{\theta,T}^2\lesssim h^{-n}.$$ There is also the global bound, summed over all clusters, fine bins, cells and time bins with bounded overlap, $$\label{w:eq-flat-weighted} \sum_{\theta,T}d_{\theta,T}^{\beta}s_{\theta,T}^2 \lesssim_B h^{-n}b_1^{2n/(n+1)}D^{-c_s}.$$ The first assertion is only a per-time-bin statement.
  - Explanation: Flattened packets satisfy a per-time-bin unweighted energy bound and a global sparse-weighted energy bound.
- **claim-tuple-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-w-tuple)): For each retained tuple, $$\label{w:eq-tuple} \lambda^2 M_0\leq CR^C(1+\textstyle\sum_i\ell_i)^C m\chi^{1-\beta}h^{n+2n/(n+1)} \left(\prod_{i=1}^n d_{\ell_i}E_i\right)^{\beta/n} \left(\sum_{i=1}^nE_i\right)^{1-\beta}.$$ The $R$ exponent is independent of the number of plate clusters and time bins and of $C_{10},C_{11}$.
  - Explanation: Each retained coarse tuple has its mass bounded using the frame estimate in one fewer dimension.
- **claim-compact-layers-bounded-frequency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-compact-layers)): Let $g\in L^2(\mathbb R^n)$ have support in a fixed bounded frequency box, and let $L\ge1$. On $\|T\|\le C L^2$, its extension is a sum of compact arrays of scale $L$, $$\mathcal E g(X,T)=\sum_{q\in\mathbb Z^n} U_q(X,T).$$ For any prescribed finite profile derivative order $J$ and any $A>0$, the arrays can be chosen with uniformly bounded $C^J$ native profiles and coefficient energies $$E_q\le C_{A,J}\langle q\rangle^{-2A}\lVert g\rVert_2^2. \label{e:layer-energy}$$ Each fixed $q$ has uniformly bounded phase-space multiplicity. Finite coefficient truncations converge uniformly on the stipulated time range, and the sum in $q$ also converges uniformly. Consequently any homogeneous weak $L^p$ estimate for these compact arrays passes to $\mathcal E g$ without a power of $L$ or a count of layers.
  - Explanation: Bounded-frequency extensions are sums of compact arrays with rapidly decaying energies, so weak estimates pass to them without loss.
- **claim-short-time-weak** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-short-time)): Let $p>2$ be the exponent in [w:weak](#w:weak). If $N\ge1$ and $f_N\in L^2$ has Fourier support in a fixed annulus dilated by $N$, then for every unit ball $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^n$ and every time interval $I$ of length $N^{-1}$, $$\left\\|\sup_{t\in I}\|S(t)f_N\|\right\\|_{L^{p,\infty}(\Omega)} \le C N^s\lVert f_N\rVert_2. \label{e:short-formula}$$ The constant is independent of the centers of $\Omega$ and $I$.
  - Explanation: Lossless short-time weak maximal estimate for frequency-localized data on intervals of inverse frequency length.
- **claim-dual-square-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-dual-square)): Fix measurable $t:\Omega\to[0,1)$ and $w:\Omega\to\mathbb C$ with $\|w\|\le1$, where $\Omega$ is a unit ball. Let $\psi$ be a smooth compactly supported annular cutoff. For $N=2^j$, $j\ge1$, and a half-open dyadic interval $I\subset[0,1)$ set $$G_N(I)(\xi)=\psi(\xi/N) \int_{\{x\in\Omega:t(x)\in I\}} e^{ix\cdot\xi-it(x)\|\xi\|^2}w(x)\,\mathrm dx, \qquad d_j(I)=N^{-2s}\lVert G_N(I)\rVert_2^2.$$ Then $$\sum_{j\ge1}d_j([0,1))\le C. \label{e:dual-square-formula}$$ The same $t$ and $w$ are used for every $j$.
  - Explanation: Normalized dual squares sum over all dyadic frequencies on one common time tree.
- **claim-local-maximal-endpoint** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-maximal)): For compactly supported $\widehat f\in L^2$ and every unit ball $\Omega$, $$\int_\Omega\sup_{0\le t<1}\|S(t)f(x)\|\,\mathrm dx \le C\lVert f\rVert_{H^s(\mathbb R^n)}. \label{e:maximal-formula}$$ The constant is uniform under translation of $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: Local endpoint maximal estimate controlled by the critical Sobolev norm with no frequency logarithm.
- **claim-gaussian-local-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-gaussian-local)): Let $M\ge0$ be measurable and $A=\sup_z\int_{B(z,1)}M<\infty$. For $$K_a(y)=(4\pi a)^{-n/2}e^{-\|y\|^2/(4a)},\qquad a>0,$$ the convolution $K_a*M$ is finite at every point. At every Lebesgue point $x$ of $M$, it tends to $M(x)$ as $a\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Gaussian averages of a locally uniformly integrable nonnegative function converge at every Lebesgue point.

```json
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed integer $n\\geq3$ and every complex-valued $f\\in H^{s_n}(\\mathbb R^n)$, there is a full-measure set $E_f$ of Lebesgue points of $f$ and a function $v(x,t)$, $x\\in E_f$, $0\\leq t<1$, such that $v(x,\\cdot)$ is continuous, $v(x,0)=f^*(x)$, and $$\\label{m:eq-uniform-Gaussian}\n \\lim_{a\\downarrow0}\\sup_{0<t<1}|U_af(x,t)-v(x,t)|=0\n \\qquad(x\\in E_f).$$ In particular the finite Gaussian limit $$u_f(x,t)=\\lim_{a\\downarrow0}U_af(x,t)$$ exists for every real $0<t<1$ on this one set, and $\\lim_{t\\downarrow0}u_f(x,t)=f^*(x)$.",

        "exact_quote": "Put $d=k+1$, $R\\geq2$, and $2\\leq p\\leq q_k$. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is $\\vartheta_0=\\vartheta_0(d,p,\\varepsilon)>0$ such that the following holds for $0<\\vartheta\\leq\\vartheta_0$ and $\\Lambda=R^\\vartheta$, with a constant allowed to depend on $(d,p,\\varepsilon,\\vartheta)$. Work in a fixed $O(R)$ test box and one common padded time slab $S$ of length $O(R)$. Every tube core meets a fixed $O(R)$ enlargement of the test box; in particular the family has polynomial cardinality. Fine frequency labels lie in a bounded patch of a translated, bounded-overlap lattice of mesh $R^{-1/2}$; the allowed class of such lattices is closed under parabolic rescaling. A fixed $O_d(1)$ number of cap grids may be separated into colors. A labelled tube is the intersection with $S$ of $$\\{(x,t):|x-2vt-u|_\\infty\\leq\\Lambda R^{1/2}\\}.$$ For $c\\geq1$, $cT$ dilates the transverse width only, in the same common slab. Tubes of a fixed label have bounded overlap before clipping by a spatial box; equivalently, their intercepts are globally separated at scale $\\Lambda R^{1/2}$ up to fixed multiplicity. Fix $1\\leq a\\leq10$ and $a+1/2\\leq b\\leq20$. Suppose the nonzero $F_T$ have comparable $L^p$ norms, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\widehat F_T\\subset\n \\{(\\xi,\\tau):|\\xi-v|\\lesssim R^{-1/2},\\\n              |\\tau+|\\xi|^2|\\lesssim\\Lambda/R\\},\\qquad\n \\|F_T\\|_{L^p(\\mathbb R^d\\setminus aT)}\\leq R^{-B}\\|F_T\\|_p,$$ where $B$ is a sufficiently large fixed number. Let $U$ be a union of $R^{1/2}$-cubes in the interior of the box and slab, each meeting at most $M\\geq1$ of the *counted* tubes $bT$. Then $$\\label{p:buffered-equation}\n \\left\\|\\sum_TF_T\\right\\|_{L^p(U)}\n \\leq C_{\\varepsilon,\\vartheta,d,p}R^\\varepsilon\n M^{1/2-1/p}\\left(\\sum_T\\|F_T\\|_p^p\\right)^{1/p}.$$ The same assertion holds for finitely many fixed cap-grid colors. The spatial boxes and tube strips may be translated; all overlap assumptions refer to the unclipped strips.",

      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p-buffered-refined",
          "exact_quote": "Put $d=k+1$, $R\\geq2$, and $2\\leq p\\leq q_k$. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is $\\vartheta_0=\\vartheta_0(d,p,\\varepsilon)>0$ such that the following holds for $0<\\vartheta\\leq\\vartheta_0$ and $\\Lambda=R^\\vartheta$, with a constant allowed to depend on $(d,p,\\varepsilon,\\vartheta)$. Work in a fixed $O(R)$ test box and one common padded time slab $S$ of length $O(R)$. Every tube core meets a fixed $O(R)$ enlargement of the test box; in particular the family has polynomial cardinality. Fine frequency labels lie in a bounded patch of a translated, bounded-overlap lattice of mesh $R^{-1/2}$; the allowed class of such lattices is closed under parabolic rescaling. A fixed $O_d(1)$ number of cap grids may be separated into colors. A labelled tube is the intersection with $S$ of $$\\{(x,t):|x-2vt-u|_\\infty\\leq\\Lambda R^{1/2}\\}.$$ For $c\\geq1$, $cT$ dilates the transverse width only, in the same common slab. Tubes of a fixed label have bounded overlap before clipping by a spatial box; equivalently, their intercepts are globally separated at scale $\\Lambda R^{1/2}$ up to fixed multiplicity. Fix $1\\leq a\\leq10$ and $a+1/2\\leq b\\leq20$. Suppose the nonzero $F_T$ have comparable $L^p$ norms, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\widehat F_T\\subset\n \\{(\\xi,\\tau):|\\xi-v|\\lesssim R^{-1/2},\\\n              |\\tau+|\\xi|^2|\\lesssim\\Lambda/R\\},\\qquad\n \\|F_T\\|_{L^p(\\mathbb R^d\\setminus aT)}\\leq R^{-B}\\|F_T\\|_p,$$ where $B$ is a sufficiently large fixed number. Let $U$ be a union of $R^{1/2}$-cubes in the interior of the box and slab, each meeting at most $M\\geq1$ of the *counted* tubes $bT$. Then $$\\label{p:buffered-equation}\n \\left\\|\\sum_TF_T\\right\\|_{L^p(U)}\n \\leq C_{\\varepsilon,\\vartheta,d,p}R^\\varepsilon\n M^{1/2-1/p}\\left(\\sum_T\\|F_T\\|_p^p\\right)^{1/p}.$$ The same assertion holds for finitely many fixed cap-grid colors. The spatial boxes and tube strips may be translated; all overlap assumptions refer to the unclipped strips.",

      "exact_statement": "Put $d=k+1$, $R\\geq2$, and $2\\leq p\\leq q_k$. Work in a fixed $O(R)$ test box and one common padded time slab $S$ of length $O(R)$. Every tube core meets a fixed $O(R)$ enlargement of the test box; in particular the family has polynomial cardinality. Fine frequency labels lie in a bounded patch of a translated, bounded-overlap lattice of mesh $R^{-1/2}$; the allowed class of such lattices is closed under parabolic rescaling. Suppose the nonzero $F_T$ have comparable $L^p$ norms, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\widehat F_T\\subset\n \\{(\\xi,\\tau):|\\xi-v|\\lesssim R^{-1/2},\\\n              |\\tau+|\\xi|^2|\\lesssim\\Lambda/R\\},\\qquad\n \\|F_T\\|_{L^p(\\mathbb R^d\\setminus aT)}\\leq R^{-B}\\|F_T\\|_p,$$ where $B$ is a sufficiently large fixed number. Let $U$ be a union of $R^{1/2}$-cubes in the interior of the box and slab, each meeting at most $M\\geq1$ of the *counted* tubes $bT$. Then $$\\label{p:buffered-equation}\n \\left\\|\\sum_TF_T\\right\\|_{L^p(U)}\n \\leq C_{\\varepsilon,\\vartheta,d,p}R^\\varepsilon\n M^{1/2-1/p}\\left(\\sum_T\\|F_T\\|_p^p\\right)^{1/p}.$$ The same assertion holds for finitely many fixed cap-grid colors.         "exact_quote": "Let $2<p\\leq q_k$, and let $f_T$ be band-limited packets of scale $N$. For $b_T\\geq0$, require $f_T=0$ if $b_T=0$, and set $B_b(z)^2=\\sum_Tb_T^2w_T(z)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p-convolution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K'\\geq K\\geq1$, let a $K'$-cap lie in a $K$-cap, and let $T'$ be a child cell of scale $K'$. If $\\mathcal G_K$ is the parent grid in that cap, then, for $P>k+2$ sufficiently large, $$\\label{p:convolution-equation}\n \\sum_{T\\in\\mathcal G_K}w_T(z)w_{T'}(z_T)\\leq C_Pw_{T'}(z).$$ The same exponent $P$ occurs on both sides, independently of $K'/K$. If the sum is restricted to $d_T(z)>L$, $L\\geq2$, it is at most $$\\label{p:convolution-tail}\n C_P\\left(L^{-(P-k-1)}+(K/K')^{P-k-2}\\right)w_{T'}(z).$$ When $d_{T'}(z)$ is bounded, the second term can be omitted.",

      },

If the sum is restricted to $d_T(z)>L$, $L\\geq2$, it is at most $$\\label{p:convolution-tail}\n C_P\\left(L^{-(P-k-1)}+(K/K')^{P-k-2}\\right)w_{T'}(z).$$ When $d_{T'}(z)$ is bounded, the second term can be omitted.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p-hierarchy",
        "exact_quote": "Take nested frequency grids at scales $K<K'$, rounded within fixed factors. Assemble all child fields in each parent cap, and localize them on its parent physical grid. The localizers can be chosen band-limited, with summable Schwartz tails and bounded sums, so that the resulting parent packets have amplitudes $a_T$ and satisfy $$\\label{p:parent-weights}\n b_T^2\\geq c\\sum_{T'\\text{ in the parent cap}}b_{T'}^2w_{T'}(z_T)$$ whenever the parent weights are chosen to dominate the displayed sum. For $2<p\\leq q_k$, $$\\label{p:hierarchy-equation}\n \\sum_T K^{k+2}a_T^p b_T^{2-p}\n \\leq C_\\varepsilon(K'/K)^\\varepsilon\n \\sum_{T'}(K')^{k+2}a_{T'}^p b_{T'}^{2-p}.$$ It remains valid after retaining any subset of parent cells, at every step of a finite hierarchy. After modifying a child field, use its fresh amplitudes in the same fixed parent masks. On subtrees lying in a codimension-one strip at their final frequency resolution one may use $p=q_{k-1}$ at every transition; for $k=1$ any fixed finite $p>2$ is available. Products of the transition estimates give the complete finite-hierarchy estimate.*\n\n*Fixed polynomial weights on macroscopic boxes, and any fixed additional edge decay, are allowed. Their orders are chosen once, before the finite grid is chosen. Constants may depend on the finite grid; the required original profile regularity and decay order do not grow with its depth.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },

        {

For $2<p\\leq q_k$, $$\\label{p:hierarchy-equation}\n \\sum_T K^{k+2}a_T^p b_T^{2-p}\n \\leq C_\\varepsilon(K'/K)^\\varepsilon\n \\sum_{T'}(K')^{k+2}a_{T'}^p b_{T'}^{2-p}.$$ It remains valid after retaining any subset of parent cells, at every step of a finite hierarchy. After modifying a child field, use its fresh amplitudes in the same fixed parent masks.         "anchor": "stmt-p-near-cauchy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V=\\sum_Tf_T$ be a band-limited field of scale $N$, and let $B^2=\\sum_Ts_T^2w_T$. Suppose a finite set $Y$ of unit cubes has witnesses $z_Q\\in Q$ such that $$B(z_Q)\\leq1,\\qquad |V(z_Q)|\\geq N^{k/2}/R,\\qquad R\\geq1.$$ Then, for every $\\varepsilon>0$, $$\\label{p:near-cauchy-equation}\n |Y|\\leq C_\\varepsilon R^{q_k+\\varepsilon}\\sum_Ts_T^2.$$ The conclusion holds with unit suprema and fixed enlargements of the witness cubes. The Fourier enlargement and original decay orders can be fixed before $\\varepsilon$ is chosen.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-p-polynomial-start",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of [p:near-cauchy](#p:near-cauchy), there is a fixed finite $M_k$ such that $$\\label{p:polynomial-start-equation}\n |Y|\\leq C(2+R)^{M_k}\\sum_Ts_T^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"

      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-frame",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$, $c>0$, and fixed packet bounds $C_0$. There is a finite integer $P_0$ such that, for every fixed $P\\ge P_0$, there are a finite integer $J_0$ and a constant $C$, depending only on $k,c,C_0,P$ and the packet conventions, with the following property. Let $h\\ge2$, let $V_1,\\ldots,V_m$ be scale-$h$ packet sums on one time interval of length at most $C_0h^2$, and suppose their normalized compact profiles have derivatives through order $J_0$ bounded by $C_0$. Let $\\Xi_i$ be their assigned frequency-label sets, with the fixed cap enlargements included. Assume bounded phase-space counting and $$\\big|\\det((2\\xi_1,1),\\ldots,(2\\xi_m,1))\\big|\\ge\\delta>0\n \\quad(\\xi_i\\in\\Xi_i).$$ Use $P$ for the packet envelope exponent. Let $Y$ be a set of distinct unit cubes and, for every $Q\\in Y$ and $i$, let $z_{i,Q}\\in Q$ satisfy $$|V_i(z_{i,Q})|\\ge A_i\\ge h^c,\n \\qquad B_i(z_{i,Q})^2=\\sum_Ts_{i,T}^2w_T(z_{i,Q})\\le1.$$ The points $z_{i,Q}$ need not agree for different $i$. Set $$H_i=A_i^{2/k},\\qquad H_g=\\Big(\\prod_{i=1}^mH_i\\Big)^{1/m},\n \\qquad D_i=h^{k+2}\\sum_Ts_{i,T}^2.$$ Then $$\\label{f:mainbound}\n |Y|\\le C\\delta^{-C}H_g^{-2}\\sum_{i=1}^m\\frac{D_i}{A_i^2}.$$ Only the finite number $J_0$ of profile derivatives is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-f-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C_3$ sufficiently large. There is a fixed $L$ such that, with $K_0=HS^L\\le h$, each cap field $V_b$ admits a scale-$K_0$ packet representation with integrated square energy at most $CD$ and envelopes $e_b^2=\\sum_{\\gamma\\in b}s_\\gamma^2w_\\gamma^{\\rm work}$ satisfying $$\\label{f:clipped}\n \\sum_b\\min(e_b^2(z),M_c)\\le S^{o(1)},\n \\qquad M_c=H^{-k}S^{C_3},$$ outside $o(|Y|)$ witnesses. The work weight $w_\\gamma^{\\rm work}=(1+d_\\gamma)^{-P_{\\rm work}}$ has a fixed order $P_{\\rm work}>P$, chosen in the proof; all subsequent levels of this subpower tree use that order. The envelope and profile orders are finite and independent of any subsequent scale-grid length. If $K_0>h$, the tree instead ends at the unchanged original scale-$h$ packets with their original order $P$; their square envelope is already bounded at the witnesses.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-f-selection",
        "exact_quote": "On a fixed finite grid one can select ratios $r_{i,s}$, retain a subpower fraction of the high cubes, and retain packet masks with the following properties. Put $$\\label{f:occupancy-size}\n M_{i,s}=\\kappa_i(s)^{k+2}(r_{i,s}/A_i)^q.$$ At every selected scale the original selected amplitudes obey $a_T\\asymp r_{i,s}b_T$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_{i,s}&:=\\sum_T|T|a_T^qb_T^{2-q}=\\Lambda^{o(1)}D_i,\n                                                        \\label{f:Qsat}\\\\\n E_{i,s}&:=\\sum_T|T|a_T^2=\\Lambda^{o(1)}D_ir_{i,s}^{2-q},\n                                                        \\label{f:Esat}\\\\\n \\sum_{T\\text{ active}}b_T^2&\\le\n             \\Lambda^{o(1)}|Y|/M_{i,s}.                  \\label{f:Bcharge}\n\\end{aligned}$$ An active cell has at most $\\Lambda^{o(1)}M_{i,s}$ eligible witnesses in its prescribed subpower enlargement. All witnesses are eligible in the power case and at strict ancestors of transition bins. Within a subpower transition bin $b$, eligibility means $e_b^2(z)\\le\\Lambda^{o(1)}M_c$.*\n\n*More generally, a collection of actual outputs in fixed masks whose weighted $q$ budget is at most $\\Lambda^{-\\varepsilon}D_i$, for fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, has negligible root contribution. The same assertion holds for error summands in any later restricted tree.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-f-profiles",
        "exact_quote": "After taking subsequences and finer finite rational grids there are nonnegative, nonincreasing, $k/2$-Lipschitz functions $f_i$ such that $$r_{i,s}=\\Lambda^{f_i(s)+o(1)},\\qquad f_i(e_i)=0.$$ In the power case $f_i(0)=\\lim\\log_h A_i$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-paths",
        "exact_quote": "For the selected tree, and for each of the directional restrictions constructed below, one can draw a uniform root point $Z$ from a subpower fraction of $Y$ and then a path to a fine direction $\\Omega_i$ in each field. The fields’ paths may be drawn independently conditional on $Z$.*\n\n\n\n*For the reference cap square weights just defined, at a typical sampled label $$\\label{f:cap-law}\n \\Pr(\\tau\\mid Z)=\\Lambda^{o(1)}b_\\tau^2(Z).$$ Let $T_s(Z,\\Omega_i)$ be the cell containing $Z$ in the disjoint scale-$\\kappa_i(s)$ lattice tilted by $\\Omega_i$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-conditioning",
        "exact_quote": "Inside a fixed cap $\\tau_l$, let $C$ be a physical cell over which every descendant velocity has drift at most the endpoint width, and whose time range is at most the endpoint duration. Adding an independently redrawn descendant direction as conditioning changes the logarithmic information of position inside $C$ by $o(1)$ at a typical draw. The cell may depend on a different conditionally independent drawn direction. The conclusion also holds for subpartitions and subpower distortions. In the power case it holds for a direction from another field whenever its full weights have the same bounded drift on $C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-f-conditioning",
          "exact_quote": "Inside a fixed cap $\\tau_l$, let $C$ be a physical cell over which every descendant velocity has drift at most the endpoint width, and whose time range is at most the endpoint duration. Adding an independently redrawn descendant direction as conditioning changes the logarithmic information of position inside $C$ by $o(1)$ at a typical draw. The cell may depend on a different conditionally independent drawn direction. The conclusion also holds for subpartitions and subpower distortions. In the power case it holds for a direction from another field whenever its full weights have the same bounded drift on $C$.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Inside a fixed cap $\\tau_l$, let $C$ be a physical cell over which every descendant velocity has drift at most the endpoint width, and whose time range is at most the endpoint duration. Adding an independently redrawn descendant direction as conditioning changes the logarithmic information of position inside $C$ by $o(1)$ at a typical draw. The cell may depend on a different conditionally independent drawn direction. The conclusion also holds for subpartitions and subpower distortions. In the power case it holds for a direction from another field whenever its full weights have the same bounded drift on $C$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-angular",
        "exact_quote": "Fix one field, a cap parameter $l$ away from the endpoints and the transition seam, and a finite mesh of $0<v\\le u$ with $l+Cu<e_i$. Suppose that $$f_i(l)-f_i(l+v)\\ge xv$$ on this mesh, for some $x>0$. For a number $\\eta>0$ depending only on $x,k$, one may restrict the frozen tree so that, at typical $(Z,\\tau_l)$, its new conditional direction law assigns mass at most $\\Lambda^{-\\eta v+o(1)}$ to every affine hyperplane strip of width $\\Lambda^{-v}$ in the native frequency coordinates of $\\tau_l$.*\n\n*At one arbitrarily small mesh precision $v_0>0$, the sampled active $l$ cells may also be required to have broad witnesses for their *original pre-modification* fields. Namely, there are $k+1$ $(l+v_0)$ cap groups, transverse throughout their supports with determinant at least $\\Lambda^{-C_kv_0}$, each having magnitude at least $\\Lambda^{-C_kv_0}r_{i,l}b_{\\tau_l}(Z)$ at a point within native distance $\\Lambda^{C_kv_0}$ of the cell.*\n\n*The high root set loses only a vanishing fraction, and Lemmas <a href=\"#f:paths\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"f:paths\">19</a> and <a href=\"#f:conditioning\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"f:conditioning\">20</a> apply to the modified tree with the same recorded occupancy sizes. This construction is made for one fixed block at a time; no simultaneous assertion over all blocks is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-inflation",
        "exact_quote": "Work inside a cap $\\tau_l$ of the preceding lemma, and suppose $f(l+v)-f(l)=-xv+O(\\varepsilon u)$ on the required finite mesh in $[0,2u]$. Put $d_0=1-2x/k$. For the isotropic cell $Q_v$ of radius $\\Lambda^v$ in this cap’s native coordinates, define the sampled effective occupancy $$N_v=\\Pr(Q_v(Z)\\mid\\tau_l)/\\Pr(Z\\mid\\tau_l).$$ For $u\\le v\\le2u$ on that mesh, $$\\label{f:inflation-occupancy}\n \\log_\\Lambda(N_v/M_l)\\le(k+1)d_0v+O(\\varepsilon u).$$ The error can be made arbitrarily small by taking the broad precision $v_0/u$ small, refining the finite mesh, and then taking the sequence limit. All spatial tests stay on one side of the transition seam.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-f-midpoint",
        "exact_quote": "For almost every pair $l<a$ of full-slope points in one field, $(l+a)/2$ is a full-slope point. Consequently each field’s full-slope set is an interval modulo null sets.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-cross-field-inflation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K\\le R\\le K^2$ be two inverse widths in a fixed finite packet tree. At scale $K$, let $W_j$, $1\\le j\\le m=k+1$, be the original selected packet fields testing the root high set, with no outer smaller-inverse-width mask. Each $W_j$ is assembled from exactly its selected scale-$R$ descendants through the frozen bounded masks and scale-$K$ localizers; no omitted branch contributes to $W_j$. The witnesses below test these same masked fields $W_j$. Suppose their frequency labels, including fixed enlargements, are $\\delta$-transverse. Write $\\mathcal E_{j,K}=\\sum_Ta_{j,T}^2w_T$ for their local square envelopes and $\\mathcal E_{j,R}$ for the envelopes of their descendants at scale $R$ in the same frozen tree. Let $Y$ be a set of distinct unit cubes with separate field witnesses at thresholds $A_j$. Suppose that, on every $K$-ball meeting $Y$, $$K^{k/2}\\mathcal E_{j,K}(z)^{1/2}/A_j\\le U,\n \\qquad 1\\le j\\le m,$$ at one, hence every, point $z$ in that ball, where $U\\ge1$. Then for every fixed $\\varepsilon>0$ and every $R$-ball centered at a surviving root point $z$, $$\\label{f:cross-field-inflation-bound}\n \\#(Y\\cap B_R(z))\n \\lesssim_{\\varepsilon,\\mathcal G}\\delta^{-C_\\varepsilon}U^\\varepsilon R^{k+1}\n \\prod_{j=1}^m\\bigl(\\mathcal E_{j,R}(z)/A_j^2\\bigr)^{1/k}.$$ The constant may depend on the fixed finite grid $\\mathcal G$, but not on the aspect ratio $R/K$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-f-cross-field-inflation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K\\le R\\le K^2$ be two inverse widths in a fixed finite packet tree. Each $W_j$ is assembled from exactly its selected scale-$R$ descendants through the frozen bounded masks and scale-$K$ localizers; no omitted branch contributes to $W_j$. The witnesses below test these same masked fields $W_j$. Suppose their frequency labels, including fixed enlargements, are $\\delta$-transverse. Write $\\mathcal E_{j,K}=\\sum_Ta_{j,T}^2w_T$ for their local square envelopes and $\\mathcal E_{j,R}$ for the envelopes of their descendants at scale $R$ in the same frozen tree. Let $Y$ be a set of distinct unit cubes with separate field witnesses at thresholds $A_j$. Suppose that, on every $K$-ball meeting $Y$, $$K^{k/2}\\mathcal E_{j,K}(z)^{1/2}/A_j\\le U,\n \\qquad 1\\le j\\le m,$$ at one, hence every, point $z$ in that ball, where $U\\ge1$. Then for every fixed $\\varepsilon>0$ and every $R$-ball centered at a surviving root point $z$, $$\\label{f:cross-field-inflation-bound}\n \\#(Y\\cap B_R(z))\n \\lesssim_{\\varepsilon,\\mathcal G}\\delta^{-C_\\varepsilon}U^\\varepsilon R^{k+1}\n \\prod_{j=1}^m\\bigl(\\mathcal E_{j,R}(z)/A_j^2\\bigr)^{1/k}.$$ The constant may depend on the fixed finite grid $\\mathcal G$, but not on the aspect ratio $R/K$.",

Then for every fixed $\\varepsilon>0$ and every $R$-ball centered at a surviving root point $z$, $$\\label{f:cross-field-inflation-bound}\n \\#(Y\\cap B_R(z))\n \\lesssim_{\\varepsilon,\\mathcal G}\\delta^{-C_\\varepsilon}U^\\varepsilon R^{k+1}\n \\prod_{j=1}^m\\bigl(\\mathcal E_{j,R}(z)/A_j^2\\bigr)^{1/k}.$$ The constant may depend on the fixed finite grid $\\mathcal G$, but not on the aspect ratio $R/K$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-d-dz-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $S\\ge1$. Suppose their counting function in every ball of radius $r\\ge1$ is at most $\\Gamma r^n$, and their number in any ball of radius $\\sqrt S$ is at most $\\lambda_0$. Then for every $\\varepsilon>0$, $$\\sum_{q\\in\\mathcal Q}\\sup_q|\\mathcal E g|^2\n \\le C_\\varepsilon S^{\\varepsilon}\n       \\Gamma^{4/((n+1)(n+2))}\\lambda_0^{\\gamma_n}\n       S^{\\gamma_n}\\lVert g\\rVert_2^2.\n \\label{d:dz-sup}$$ All fixed enlargements and translations of the cube lattice are allowed, with fixed changes of the constant.",

      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $S\\ge1$.           "source_id": "PM-080.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $S\\ge1$.
```
