<a id="stmt-e-maximal"></a>
<div id="e:maximal" class="theorem">

<a id="e:maximal-formula"></a>
**Theorem 63** (Local $L^1$ endpoint maximal estimate). *For compactly supported $\widehat f\in L^2$ and every unit ball $\Omega$, $$\int_\Omega\sup_{0\le t<1}|S(t)f(x)|\,\mathrm dx
       \le C\lVert f\rVert_{H^s(\mathbb R^n)}.
 \label{e:maximal-formula}$$ The constant is uniform under translation of $\Omega$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-e-maximal"><summary>Proof of stmt-e-maximal</summary>

*Proof.* Take a smooth inhomogeneous Littlewood–Paley decomposition $f=f_{\mathrm{lo}}+\sum_{j\ge1}f_{2^j}$ with bounded frequency overlap. The low-frequency part satisfies $\sup_{x,t}|S(t)f_{\mathrm{lo}}(x)|\le C\lVert f_{\mathrm{lo}}\rVert_2$. For a fixed measurable graph $t(x)$ and phase $w(x)$, frequency Cauchy–Schwarz and [e:dual-square](#e:dual-square) give $$\begin{aligned}
 \left|\int_\Omega w(x)\sum_jS(t(x))f_{2^j}(x)\,\mathrm dx\right|
 &\le C\left(\sum_j2^{2js}\lVert f_{2^j}\rVert_2^2\right)^{1/2}
        \left(\sum_j2^{-2js}\lVert G_{2^j}([0,1))\rVert_2^2\right)^{1/2}\\
 &\le C\lVert f\rVert_{H^s}.
\end{aligned}$$ Choose the annular cutoff in $G_{2^j}$ equal to one on the corresponding Littlewood–Paley support. The first factor is the Sobolev square sum; no frequency logarithm or $\ell^1$ sum has appeared. For a finite time list, select the first maximizer of the full field and its conjugate phase (zero where the field is zero). These are measurable and are common to every frequency. The preceding bound is then the integral of that finite maximum. Increase the lists to a dense countable subset of $[0,1)$ containing zero. Continuity of the bounded-frequency field and monotone convergence prove <a href="#e:maximal-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="e:maximal-formula">[e:maximal-formula]</a>. All kernel estimates depend only on spatial differences, giving the asserted translation uniformity. ◻

</details>

<a id="e:trace"></a>
#### One representative and all real times

To complete [m:main](#m:main), we first construct a representative continuous in time and then remove Gaussian regularization uniformly in time on one full-measure spatial set. The following approximate-identity lemma will be applied to a countable family of error envelopes.

<a id="stmt-e-gaussian-local"></a>
<div id="e:gaussian-local" class="lemma">

**Lemma 64** (Gaussian approximation with uniform local integrals). *Let $M\ge0$ be measurable and $A=\sup_z\int_{B(z,1)}M<\infty$. For $$K_a(y)=(4\pi a)^{-n/2}e^{-|y|^2/(4a)},\qquad a>0,$$ the convolution $K_a*M$ is finite at every point. At every Lebesgue point $x$ of $M$, it tends to $M(x)$ as $a\downarrow0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-e-gaussian-local"><summary>Proof of stmt-e-gaussian-local</summary>

<a id="e:gaussian-annuli"></a>
*Proof.* For a fixed $\rho>0$, cover each annulus $2^\ell\rho\le|y|<2^{\ell+1}\rho$ by $C(1+2^\ell\rho)^n$ unit balls. Uniformly in the center $x$, $$\int_{|y|\ge\rho}K_a(y)M(x-y)\,\mathrm dy
 \le C A a^{-n/2}\sum_{\ell\ge0}(1+2^\ell\rho)^n
                    e^{-4^\ell\rho^2/(4a)}.
 \label{e:gaussian-annuli}$$ The series is finite for every $a>0$, and for all sufficiently small $a$ it is at most $C_\rho A a^{-n/2}e^{-\rho^2/(8a)}$. The integral over a bounded ball is finite by local integrability, which proves the first assertion.

At a Lebesgue point set $H_x(r)=\int_{|y|<r}|M(x-y)-M(x)|\,\mathrm dy=o(r^n)$. Given $\varepsilon>0$, choose $\rho$ so that $H_x(r)\le\varepsilon r^n$ for $0<r\le\rho$. Radial integration by parts gives $$\int_{|y|<\rho}K_a(y)|M(x-y)-M(x)|\,\mathrm dy
 \le \varepsilon\left(K_a(\rho)\rho^n+
                  \int_0^\rho(-K_a'(r))r^n\,\mathrm dr\right)
 \le C\varepsilon.$$ The complementary integral tends to zero by <a href="#e:gaussian-annuli" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="e:gaussian-annuli">[e:gaussian-annuli]</a>, together with the elementary Gaussian tail bound for the constant $|M(x)|$. Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ proves the claim. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-e-gaussian-local-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-e-gaussian-local</summary>

*Proof of [m:main](#m:main).* Fix $n\ge3$ and a complex-valued $f\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)$. Choose $f_j$ with $\widehat f_j\in C_c^\infty$ and $\lVert f_j-f\rVert_{H^s}\le2^{-j}$. The successive differences satisfy a summable $H^s$ bound. By [e:maximal](#e:maximal), on every unit ball $$\int\sum_j\sup_{0\le t<1}|S(t)(f_{j+1}-f_j)(x)|\,\mathrm dx<\infty.$$ A countable covering of $\mathbb R^n$ therefore gives a common full-measure set $E_0$ on which $S(t)f_j(x)$ converges uniformly for $0\le t<1$. Call the limit $v(x,t)$ and set $v(x,t)=0$ off $E_0$. For every $x$, $v(x,\cdot)$ is continuous on $[0,1)$; it is jointly measurable as a pointwise limit on a measurable set. The measurability of $E_0$ and of all the suprema just used can equally be read from the countable dense set of times, by continuity.

<a id="e:class-gaussian-identity"></a>
Fix an arbitrary real $t\in[0,1)$. Unitarity gives $S(t)f_j\to S(t)f$ in $L^2$, whereas the construction gives pointwise convergence to $v(\cdot,t)$ almost everywhere. Hence $v(\cdot,t)$ is an $L^2$ representative of $S(t)f$. For each $a>0$ and $x\in\mathbb R^n$, the function $K_a(x-\cdot)$ lies in $L^2$. Pairing it with this $L^2$ class and using Plancherel yields the everywhere-defined identity $$U_a f(x,t)=(K_a*v(\cdot,t))(x).
 \label{e:class-gaussian-identity}$$ The fixed $t$ was arbitrary. Thus <a href="#e:class-gaussian-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="e:class-gaussian-identity">[e:class-gaussian-identity]</a> holds for every triple $(x,a,t)$ in the indicated ranges; no intersection of time-dependent exceptional sets has been taken. The convolution is absolutely convergent by Cauchy–Schwarz.

Let $$M_j(x)=\sup_{0\le t<1}|v(x,t)-S(t)f_j(x)|.$$ These functions are measurable because the supremum equals the one over rational times. They tend to zero on $E_0$. Uniform convergence there and Fatou’s lemma applied to $\sup_t|S(t)(f_\ell-f_j)|$ show that <a id="e:envelope-local-integrals"></a>$$A_j:=\sup_z\int_{B(z,1)}M_j(x)\,\mathrm dx
       \le C\lVert f-f_j\rVert_{H^s}.
 \label{e:envelope-local-integrals}$$ All translated balls have the same constant by [e:maximal](#e:maximal). The values on the null complement of $E_0$ do not affect this estimate. Intersect $E_0$ with the Lebesgue-point sets of the countably many $M_j$. By [e:gaussian-local](#e:gaussian-local), at every point of this intersection $K_a*M_j(x)\to M_j(x)$ for every $j$.

<a id="e:envelope-gaussian-bound"></a>
For every $y$ and every $t$, the definition of $M_j$ gives the simultaneous inequality $|v(y,t)-S(t)f_j(y)|\le M_j(y)$. Combining it with <a href="#e:class-gaussian-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="e:class-gaussian-identity">[e:class-gaussian-identity]</a> gives $$\sup_{0<t<1}|U_a f(x,t)-v(x,t)|
 \le (K_a*M_j)(x)+M_j(x)+R_j(a),
 \label{e:envelope-gaussian-bound}$$ where $$R_j(a):=(2\pi)^{-n}
     \int |1-e^{-a|\xi|^2}|\,|\widehat f_j(\xi)|\,\mathrm d\xi
       \longrightarrow0.$$ This last convergence is uniform in $x,t$ since $\widehat f_j\in L^1$. First let $a\downarrow0$ at a point of the common set. The limsup of the left side of <a href="#e:envelope-gaussian-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="e:envelope-gaussian-bound">[e:envelope-gaussian-bound]</a> is at most $2M_j(x)$. Then let $j\to\infty$. This proves <a href="#m:eq-uniform-Gaussian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq-uniform-Gaussian">[m:eq-uniform-Gaussian]</a> for all real times in the interval at once.

Finally, intersect with the Lebesgue points of $f$ and with the full-measure set on which $v(x,0)=f(x)$, supplied by the fixed-time $L^2$ identity at zero. On the resulting $E_f$, $v(x,0)=f^*(x)$ and time continuity gives $v(x,t)\to f^*(x)$ as real $t\downarrow0$. The Gaussian limit in <a href="#m:eq-uniform-Gaussian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq-uniform-Gaussian">[m:eq-uniform-Gaussian]</a> is taken first, so these are exactly the asserted iterated limits. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The forced amplitude-ratio profiles $r_{i, s} = Λ^{f_{i}(s) + o(1)}$. Their shapes follow from , [f:power-end], and . The panels show generic cases; the terminal flat segment may collapse at $s_{H} = 1$ or $e_{i} = 0$, respectively. The subpower domain continues to the left without a finite endpoint. The recomputed transition ratio is $Λ^{o(1)}$ in both cases, as used in the final incidence contradiction.",
      "figure_id": "f:profiles-figure",
      "pdf_page": 45,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "f:profiles-figure",
          "exact_quote": "The forced amplitude-ratio profiles\n $r_{i,s}=\\Lambda^{f_i(s)+o(1)}$. Their shapes follow from\n \\Cref{f:profiles,f:binary,f:midpoint}, \\eqref{f:power-end}, and\n \\Cref{f:left-limit}. The panels show generic cases; the terminal\n flat segment ",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,\n  axis/.style={-{Stealth[length=1.6mm]},black!65,line width=.5pt},\n  profile/.style={blue!65!black,line width=1.2pt},\n  every node/.style={font=\\small}]\n \\begin{scope}\n  \\node at (1.65,3.15) {Power case: $\\Lambda=h$};\n  \\node at (1.65,2.72) {$\\kappa_i(s)=h^s$};\n  \\draw[axis] (-.15,0)--(3.85,0) node[right] {$s$};\n  \\draw[axis] (0,-.15)--(0,2.45) node[above left] {$f_i(s)$};\n  \\draw[profile] (0,2.1)--(2.25,0)--(3.5,0);\n  \\fill[blue!65!black] (0,2.1) circle (1.6pt)\n                         (2.25,0) circle (1.6pt)\n                         (3.5,0) circle (1.6pt);\n  \\node[below] at (0,-.06) {$0$};\n  \\node[below] at (2.25,-.06) {$s_H$};\n  \\node[below] at (3.5,-.06) {$1$};\n  \\node[above right] at (.85,1.38) {slope $-k/2$};\n  \\node at (1.65,-.75) {$f_i(s)=\\dfrac{k}{2}(s_H-s)_+$};\n  \\node at (1.65,-1.35) {$0\\le s\\le1,\\quad0<s_H\\le1$};\n \\end{scope}\n \\begin{scope}[xshift=8.15cm]\n  \\node at (0,3.15) {Subpower case: $\\Lambda=S$};\n  \\node at (0,2.72) {$\\kappa_i(s)=H_iS^s$};\n  \\draw[axis] (-2.6,0)--(2.05,0) node[right] {$s$};\n  \\draw[axis] (0,-.15)--(0,2.45);\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (.2,2.1) {$f_i(s)$};\n  \\draw[profile,{Stealth[length=2mm]}-] (-2.25,2.1)--(0,0)--(1.5,0);\n  \\fill[blue!65!black] (0,0) circle (1.6pt)\n                         (1.5,0) circle (1.6pt);\n  \\node[below] at (0,-.06) {$0$};\n  \\node[below] at (1.5,-.06) {$e_i$};\n  \\node at (-1.3,2.3) {slope $-k/2$};\n  \\node at (0,-.75) {$f_i(s)=(-ks/2)_+$};\n  \\node at (0,-1.35) {$-\\infty<s\\le e_i,\\quad e_i\\ge0$};\n \\end{scope}\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Two side-by-side plots of amplitude-ratio profiles:\n- Left panel (\"Power case: $\\Lambda = h$\", $\\kappa_i(s) = h^s$): A Cartesian coordinate system with horizontal axis $s$ marked at $0$, $s_H$, and $1$, and vertical axis $f_i(s)$ pointing upwards at $s = 0$. A line segment descends with \"slope $-k/2$\" from $(0, f_i(0))$ to $(s_H, 0)$ on the horizontal axis, followed by a flat blue segment along the axis from $s_H$ to $1$. Below the axes are the formula $f_i(s) = \\frac{k}{2}(s_H - s)_+$ and domain $0 \\le s \\le 1,\\ 0 < s_H \\le 1$.\n- Right panel (\"Subpower case: $\\Lambda = S$\", $\\kappa_i(s) = H_i S^s$): A Cartesian coordinate system with horizontal axis $s$ marked at $0$ and $e_i$, and vertical axis $f_i(s)$ pointing upwards at $s = 0$. A line descends from the top left ($s < 0$) with \"slope $-k/2$\" to the origin $(0, 0)$, followed by a flat blue segment along the horizontal axis ending at a point labeled $e_i$. Below the axes are the formula $f_i(s) = (-ks/2)_+$ and domain $-\\infty < s \\le e_i,\\ e_i \\ge 0$."
    },
    {
      "caption": "A heavier-child path starting at absolute depth $j$. Its lighter child at relative depth $k$ has a parent at absolute depth $j + k − 1$. Holding $k$ fixed and changing the frequency index $j$ therefore selects distinct parent vertices in one common tree. The path stops at $k = j$, where the rescaled frequency is $Nr = 1$.",
      "figure_id": "e:time-tree-figure",
      "pdf_page": 92,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "e:time-tree-figure",
          "exact_quote": "A heavier-child path starting at absolute depth $j$.  Its\n lighter child at relative depth $k$ has a parent at absolute depth\n $j+k-1$.  Holding $k$ fixed and changing the frequency index $j$\n therefore selects distinct parent vertices in o",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,\n  dot/.style={circle,fill=black,inner sep=1.8pt},\n  heavy/.style={line width=1pt,blue!65!black},\n  light/.style={line width=.6pt,black!55},\n  every node/.style={font=\\small}]\n \\node[dot,label=above:{$I\\in\\mathcal D_j$}] (i) at (0,0) {};\n \\node[dot] (h1) at (.8,-.8) {};\n \\node[dot,label=left:{$P(I,1)$}] (l1) at (-1.1,-.8) {};\n \\draw[heavy] (i)--(h1); \\draw[light] (i)--(l1);\n \\node at (1.15,-1.28) {$\\vdots$};\n \\draw[heavy,densely dotted] (h1)--(1.55,-1.75);\n \\node[dot,label=above right:{$H(I,k-1)$}] (hk) at (1.55,-1.9) {};\n \\node[dot,label=below left:{$P(I,k)$}] (lk) at (.4,-2.8) {};\n \\node[dot] (hnext) at (2.45,-2.8) {};\n \\draw[light] (hk)--(lk); \\draw[heavy] (hk)--(hnext);\n \\draw[heavy,densely dotted] (hnext)--(3,-3.5);\n \\node[dot,label=below:{$H(I,j)$}] (end) at (3.15,-3.7) {};\n \\node[anchor=west] at (4.1,0) {absolute depth $j$};\n \\node[anchor=west] at (4.1,-1.9) {absolute depth $j+k-1$};\n \\node[anchor=west] at (4.1,-2.8) {relative depth $k$,\\quad $r=2^{-k}$};\n \\node[anchor=west] at (4.1,-3.7) {relative depth $j$,\\quad $Nr=1$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A tree graph illustrating branching along a path:\n- A root node at the top represented by a solid black dot is labeled $I \\in \\mathcal{D}_j$, annotated to the right as \"absolute depth $j$\".\n- A branch extends down and to the right to an intermediate node labeled $H(I, k - 1)$, annotated to the right as \"absolute depth $j + k - 1$\".\n- On the left, a node labeled $P(I, 1)$ is shown, followed downward by a vertical ellipsis of three dots, leading to a node labeled $P(I, k)$, which is connected by a branch from $H(I, k - 1)$ and annotated to the right as \"relative depth $k, \\quad r = 2^{-k}$\".\n- A thick blue path continues downward and to the right from $H(I, k - 1)$ to a leaf node labeled $H(I, j)$, annotated to the right as \"relative depth $j, \\quad Nr = 1$\"."
    }

      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The higher dimensional theorem establishes almost everywhere convergence of the regularized evolution at the critical Sobolev regularity across all higher dimensions, extending the planar endpoint convergence theorem to arbitrary dimensions.",
      "relation": "generalises",
      "source_anchor": "m:main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-080.2",
      "source_quote": "there is a full-measure set",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-080.1",
      "target_quote": "of full Lebesgue measure such that",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "symmetric",
      "explanation": "Both works utilize Gaussian regularization parameters to define pointwise limits and construct continuous spacetime representatives for the free evolution.",
      "relation": "shared_technique",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-m-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-080.2",
      "source_quote": "the finite Gaussian limit $$u_f(x,t)=\\lim_{a\\downarrow0}U_af(x,t)$$ exists",

      "target_pm_id": "PM-080.1",
      "target_quote": "the finite limit $$u_f(x,t)=\\lim_{a\\downarrow0}U_af(x,t)$$ exists for every real",

    }
